That instance indexed every one of its attachments locally after the profile was unsynced, so **local indexing assigns no version** — the nonzero values on the production instance (up to 35,545, in the *library* version space, whose value in the test database is 35,774) are stale numbers left by a sync that is now off. Two consequences follow and they point the same way: `fulltext?since=N` for any `N ≥ 1` returns **nothing at all** on the test instance while 1,115 entries exist, and recording each entry's version and diffing it later inherits exactly that blindness, because a re-index moves the version from 0 to 0. So this layer ignores `version` entirely and compares the `sha256` it already records against a fresh read. That is affordable rather than clever: fetching the whole corpus is **26 seconds and 97 MB across 1,367 attachments** (measured 2026-09-07, 19 ms per attachment), so a full pass needs no incremental mechanism to guard it. Attachment `md5` and `mtime` are available as a cheaper gate — present on 1,311 of 1,312 attachments (§9) — and are not used, because a 26-second pass does not need one and a gate that can be wrong is worse than a scan that cannot.

**Page boundaries survive.** The served text carries form feeds at page breaks — verified 2026-09-07, `content.split('\f')` yields exactly `indexedPages` segments in order. Page attribution therefore needs no second extraction pass and no PDF library, which is what the foundation spec's §5 quote-provenance machinery had assumed.

### 3.7 API use (measured 2026-09-04)

- Zotero 10.0.1 local API, version 3, serves item JSON with `key`, `version`, `citationKey`, `dateModified`, `relations`; children with `md5` and `mtime`; `/file/view/url`; per-attachment `fulltext`; `fulltext?since=`; `?since=&format=versions`; `items/trash`; a `Zotero-Server-ID` header on every response. It has no `/deleted` endpoint — re-measured 2026-09-07 on **two** instances, so this is a property of the local API and not of one database's state, and deletion detection is therefore a key-set diff or nothing.

- **The local API mints its own version numbers.** The versions it serves are not the ones in the database behind it: measured 2026-09-07, the test instance's API reports item versions in 0–1,681 while its own `items.version` column runs to 14,595 and its library sits at 35,774. Zotero's instruction to partition cached data by server id is therefore not advice about two libraries drifting — the numbers are per-instance by construction, which is why §3.2's provenance tuple carries the id, not the version alone.

- Every translator-based format on the local API (`format=csljson`, `format=bibtex`, `include=csljson`) returns HTTP 500 on 10.0.1 — measured 2026-09-04. **Measured again 2026-09-13 by Part A's Task 16 live leg: `GET /api/users/0/items/top?format=csljson&limit=1` answers 200 with a CSL JSON body on both 10.0.1 and 10.0.2.** A route observed closed and open within nine days is not a basis to build on; decision 13 stands, Better BibTeX `item.export` stays the CSL renderer, and doctor's `translator-formats` probe reports the route's state as a warning (Part A Task 16; Part B Task 5 re-measures and records).

- **A shipped path is dead on this stack.** `research_vault/zotero.py`'s `export_csl(None)` — the whole-library branch — pages `/api/users/0/items/top?format=csljson`, which is a translator format, so it raises `local API HTTP 500`. The per-citekey branch goes through Better BibTeX `item.export` and works. §3.2 regenerates the file from the captured set, not the library, so the branch has no caller in this design; it is recorded because it is live in the tree today.

- Better BibTeX renders the CSL file through the whole-library HTTP route rather than JSON-RPC (§3.3 step 4). Its JSON-RPC surface is used twice more: as the annotation fallback (§3.3 step 2) and for doctor's `api.ready` probe (§5). Its auto-export is not used, and `item.regenerate_key` is never called, because it mutates (§3.5). `item.attachments` is the named fallback for reading annotations.

- The Zotero web API is not used, for three reasons. They are not the same API at two base URLs: Zotero's local-API page exists to enumerate *"the notable differences from the Web API"*, and lists nine.

  **The surface differs.** `format=atom` returns 501. `q` uses Zotero's local quicksearch, so *"the set of items returned by a given `q` value may not match the Web API exactly"*. The local API *"does not impose a default or maximum limit"* and *"does not currently apply rate limiting"* — both in our favour, and both meaning code written against the Web API's ceilings is dead weight here. It answers 403 when the local-API preference is off, and 501 for an unsupported API version. And *"in Zotero 10+, local versions are unrelated to the versions in this API, so clients must partition cached data by server ID"* — `must`, the strongest modal on the page.

  **It also serves things the Web API does not**, and one of them is load-bearing: `<userOrGroupPrefix>/items/<itemKey>/file` and `/file/view/url` are **local-only**. §3.6's path shim and §9's `file:///D:/Zotero/storage/…` probe both depend on that endpoint, so repointing the base URL at `api.zotero.org` does not degrade the design — it breaks the attachment path outright. **Device independence is therefore an architecture, not a configuration change**. `searches/<searchKey>/items` is likewise local-only: the Web API exposes search metadata but never executes a search.

  **The data differs too**, and that half stands. `extensions.zotero.sync.fulltext.enabled` is **false** and `sync.storage.protocol` is **webdav** (measured 2026-09-07), so no extracted text was ever uploaded and the web endpoint returns nothing for the one input capture most needs. Flipping that preference would fix the data half and leave the surface half untouched.

- The bib-file export is not used.

### 3.8 The sourcing screen for capture (2026-09-07)

The 2026-09-04 draft screened compile against 43 candidates and built capture without screening it. `docs/agents/sourcing.md` puts capture in the **mature tool** tier, where a `build` verdict must name the numbered must no candidate met. This is that record.

**The bounded question.** What can project a Zotero item into a vault markdown note, supplying everything §3.2's record home needs?

**Floors.** Four — C1, C2 and C4 below, plus **C3**, promoted from scored on 2026-09-07 for the reason given with the verdict. After the author cut four from a first draft that had encoded the incumbent's shape as the requirement:

- **C1** — reads the library programmatically, no human export step.
- **C2** — yields the **Zotero item key** and the citation key distinguishably. Without both, §3.5's re-key is indistinguishable from a new source.
- **C4** — yields all item metadata byte-faithful, `Extra` included.

Scored, not floors: **C5** tags; **C6** whether the tool's own note could be adopted wholesale, which scores *highest* — adopting their note is more adoption, not less.

**Candidates screened: 17**. **Its C1–C6 are this screen's own, and do not carry the meanings the note's earlier C1–C6 give them** — that note screened the *compile* step and numbered a different criterion set, so the identifiers collide and the definitions below govern here. From the Obsidian community registry (7,367 plugins, 60 matching), the GitHub API, and four community workflow guides. Zotero-side plugins, Obsidian-side plugins, Zotero translators, standalone CLIs and a starter vault. Twelve of the 17 failed one of the three original floors, C1, C2 or C4; every one of those failures was adversarially verified and 11 survived that check, the twelfth being withdrawn. The screen itself is recorded in `docs/research/2026-09-04-import-sourcing.md`.

**Four clear C1, C2 and C4:** `obsidian-zotero-integration` (Better BibTeX JSON-RPC), `aidenlx/zotlit` (SQLite direct), `vanakat/zotero-bridge` (local API, but a 526-line library that writes no note), `wenzhi-ding/zotero-direct` (raw file read with a WAL overlay).

**None clears C3. That is the verdict's whole basis.**

| Candidate                     | Full text                                                      |
| ----------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `obsidian-zotero-integration` | zero hits for `fulltext`, `pdftotext`, `extractText` in `src/` |
| `aidenlx/zotlit`              | never reads `.zotero-ft-cache`, where the extracted text lives |
| `vanakat/zotero-bridge`       | excludes attachments outright — `itemType: '-attachment'`      |
| `wenzhi-ding/zotero-direct`   | no full text, and no annotations either                        |

**C3, attachment full text, is a floor** (promoted 2026-09-07). Capture must hand the compile step extracted text, because the adopted engine has one implemented adapter and cannot read a PDF (§4.3), so a component that cannot supply text cannot do capture's job at all. Scoring it was the error the verdict then inherited.

**Verdict: `build`, on floor C3.** No candidate supplies it, and four things make building the right answer rather than the residual one. Reading Zotero's index over a REST API is **simpler than implementing PDF-to-text**, which the T7 tracer measured as better and not merely equivalent. The result is **a small script that is not expected to evolve**, because the surface it reads is a versioned REST API. **No candidate offered functionality we lack** — each brought complexity and workarounds without adding capability. And **"captured" is a fact about this vault's evidence layer that nothing external knows**, the same reason §4.4's lint is ours to build. Zotero's own index does, and **tracer T7 measured that it beats the alternative rather than merely substituting for it**: against independent PyMuPDF extraction of five random papers, character parity 0.99–1.00, and the entire word-recall gap is PyMuPDF's hyphenation fragments against Zotero's correctly rejoined words. Zotero resolves soft hyphens across line breaks; the library does not.

Once that client exists, the rest is the same round trip. `/items/<key>`, `/children`, `/fulltext`, `?format=versions` and `items/trash` are one version-checked read pass, so metadata and the provenance tuple cost almost nothing beyond the text that had to be fetched anyway.

**Recorded so they are not re-screened.** `argenos/zotero-mdnotes` is archived, frozen at 2022, and its maintainer declined Zotero 7. `hans/obsidian-citation-plugin` has not shipped since 2022. `anoopkcn/obsidian-reference-map` is within-note and stale. `mgmeyers/pdfannots2json` is **Zotero-orthogonal** — it parses the PDF's own `/Annots` arrays, and Zotero 7+ keeps annotations in `zotero.sqlite`. `daeh/zotero-markdb-connect` flows Obsidian→Zotero, writes one tag and no files, and is a **complement to any generator including this one**, not a candidate. Two repositories in this ecosystem carry a recent `pushed_at` over frozen code and were silently archived, so activity was judged on the last commit touching source.

**What building costs, priced rather than left implicit.** The maintenance of a client against a versioned REST API, which is the low-churn surface — ZotLit needs daily maintenance precisely because it reads a private SQLite schema instead. No user-editable template, which this vault does not want, having one shape. No annotation image extraction, deferred with annotations. And no offline read: §0 defers substrate absence, and ZotLit's ability to read with Zotero closed is the capability that deferral postpones.

**What no longer counts against building.** ~~The two APIs are one API at two base URLs, so device independence is a configuration change.~~ **Withdrawn 2026-09-07 — §3.7 measures the opposite**: the surfaces differ, and `/file/view/url` is local-only, so repointing the base URL breaks the attachment path outright. Device independence is an architecture, and this line does not survive as an argument for building; the four in the verdict do. `zotero-api-client`, the JavaScript client ZotFlow builds on, already exposes `apiScheme` and `apiAuthorityPart`; ZotFlow, one of the screened candidates above, simply never passes them.

## 4. Compile

Chosen outputs (2026-09-04): a source page and cross-source concept pages, both in the adopted wiki's own shape. Claims with locators are dropped as a vault syntax; the requirement that a quoted passage be traceable to a page and byte-checkable on demand is carried by Zotero annotations captured at ingest. Charting belongs to the scoping review and is not a compile output, so the earlier requirement D2 (a caller-supplied per-source template) leaves this set and moves to the scoping review spec.

### 4.1 Requirement set the adoption was screened against

Floors: D1 a per-source page; D3 concept pages with an index, an append-only log, and contradictions kept rather than resolved; D4 the capture of the source file is separable from compiling it, so the tool accepts a raw file another process wrote; D6 installable as a Claude Code plugin or skill as-is; D10 a licence that permits use and modification. Not floors: D5 idempotent re-compile; D7 works on an existing layout or states its own; D8 references a source by a caller-supplied stable id; D9 documents its human gate.

The evidence is in `docs/research/2026-09-04-import-sourcing.md`: 43 candidates read after a triage of 295 discoveries, with every drop counted by reason. The candidate descriptions below are drawn from that note's records. Where its adversarial verify pass had not confirmed a row when this section was written, the note marks it, not this document.

### 4.2 Candidates that meet every floor as-is

Two Claude Code plugins meet D1, D3, D4, D6, and D10 without modification. Both are MIT, both were pushed in the last ten days of August 2026, both are Python underneath.

**AgriciDaniel/claude-obsidian** (v2.1.1, pin `ad67087`, 14,631 stars, 42 commits in 90 days). Integration is a transaction: read-only workers propose a bundle of page writes with expected content hashes, the person approves the plan by its hash, and an engine applies it atomically or not at all. Source pages carry key claims; a claims ledger marks contested claims and refuses contradictory evidence without an adjudication note. Sources enter through a configured inbox directory, and paths outside the vault are refused. Layout: a `wiki/` directory beside an `.obsidian/` directory. The ledger key is a content hash, so the ledger id is a content hash and carries no citation key. Neither does the locator, which §4.5 sets to `fulltext/<attachment key>.md`. **The citation key reaches the compiled layer through the page properties alone**, which is why §4.4's lint reads the pages rather than the ledger. Components at the pin: 15 skills, 3 agents, a SessionStart and a Stop hook, and the `claude_obsidian/` Python engine the skills call. `plugin.json` declares no components, so component discovery is by directory.

**nvk/llm-wiki** (v0.24.4, pin `7c94c9b`, 1,034 stars). Ingest and compile are separate commands: ingest writes an immutable raw page with a fixed six-key frontmatter, compile turns uncompiled raw pages into concept, topic, and reference pages, with a nudge at five uncompiled sources. A raw page written by another process is accepted if it carries those keys, which is the capture-then-compile split of §1 exactly. Layout: `raw/<type>/` and `wiki/`, imposed by a lint that relocates files by their frontmatter. Components: 1 skill (403 lines plus 22 reference files), 28 commands, no hooks.

Candidates that fell on a floor, for the record: ussumant/llm-wiki-compiler (Claude and Codex plugin, no per-source page); kfchou/wiki-skills (contradictions must be resolved before commit, the opposite of D3); pumblus/okf-harness (OKF-native, Apache-2.0, no append-only log by design); the hermes-agent llm-wiki skill (a Hermes skill, not a Claude Code one; its schema template is the strongest pattern donor); atomicstrata/llm-wiki-compiler and SwarmVault (Node CLIs, not plugins); llmwikis.org (prompts and a starter bundle, scripts without a licence).

### 4.3 Adoption (**ratified 2026-09-07**): claude-obsidian unmodified, driven by a wrapper

The compile step adopts AgriciDaniel/claude-obsidian at pin `ad67087`, installed whole and configured through its own mode system, with no edit to the tool. Every accommodation is on this vault's side, which is where nothing binds.

**The verdict is `adopt`, and it survived a serious attempt to make it `adapt`.** The tool's Python core does two things — renders a template tree once, and validates agent-authored transaction bundles against path-scope, hash-precondition and schema rules. Everything else (page bodies, frontmatter values, index entries, `sources:` backlinks) is written by an LLM following prose in `skills/`, `agents/` and `WIKI.md`, which invites vendoring that prose and bending the methodology toward PRISMA-S. **The upstream release model forbids it economically.** Measured at the pin: of 49 prose files **zero were unchanged across 90 days**; turnover is 2.06× overall and **2.85× on the mixed methodology files worth owning**; and **98.5% of that churn arrived in one release-promotion commit** of 81,000+ lines, because the public repository is a distribution mirror populated wholesale from a private tree. A fork re-derives its edits against a rewritten tree every release — roughly 2,800 conflicting lines per cycle on the subset worth adapting. No override seam exists: "custom scaffold" is prose pointing at prose, `extensions.py` is legacy compatibility, modes are a closed four-value enum, and no config key names a prose file.

**So research-vault drives it through a wrapper of its own** (§4.5) — glue tier under `docs/agents/sourcing.md`, the low bar — and upgrading is `git pull` with no conflict surface. Two consequences. **The methodology question defers to lane 5, and the churn is the weaker of two reasons.** The stronger one is that it may not be ours to answer. Lane 4 curates research skills — MedSci, K-Dense — and screens them against exactly the floors this methodology would be bent toward: PRISMA-S, PRISMA-ScR, the ACM guidelines. If a curated skill carries the method, we adopt it rather than writing it into a forked prose surface, and the tool's own generic knowledge-wiki practice never needs touching. Bending it now would risk building what lane 4 supplies. Lane 5's gap pass is where that resolves either way: it walks the obligations index and sources only what nothing answers, which is the shape this question already has. And **the ledger points at what capture already wrote**, `fulltext/<attachment key>.md`, which `ledgers.py` already accepts as a vault-relative canonical path — the `inbox/`-or-`.raw/` restriction is instruction, not validation. No third copy of the text is made, and the ledger's `content_sha256` is the provenance tuple's number by construction.

**One constraint a later lane should not rediscover**: `provenance.md`'s authority tiers and review states are **closed enums in `ledgers.py:25-49`**, code-validated, so PRISMA-S screening stages cannot live in the source ledger without patching Python. They belong in free-form frontmatter values and page structure, which the tool permits — it checks that six keys exist and never validates what they hold.

**The plugin arrives whole or not at all.** Curating it down is unreachable, measured against Claude Code 2.1.260: it ships a `plugin.json` at its repo root, so a marketplace entry that also lists components is rejected — *"has conflicting manifests: both plugin.json and marketplace entry specify components"* — and at `strict: true` the list supplements directory discovery rather than replacing it (*"Custom paths supplement default directories - they don't replace them"*), so all fifteen skills, three agents and both hooks load anyway. A `git-subdir` source pointed at `skills/` would curate but severs the `claude_obsidian/` engine, and every skill hard-gates on `$PRODUCT_ROOT/scripts/claude-obsidian.py`. So §4.2's component inventory is what a session carries. Two subsetting routes exist as fallbacks, neither proposed, because the surplus is a context cost, not a correctness one: **vendoring** the wanted skills under a provenance header drops the hooks by geometry but drags the engine along (~50 files, a megabyte); **an in-tree marketplace entry** works because the loader's subtraction branch fires when `source` is `"./"`, confirmed by a probe on this vault's geometry, at the price that the entry then governs this vault's own skills too. Cheaper still if the surplus bites: `permissions.deny` on a skill namespace mutes execution, though the skill still loads and the context cost remains.

**Modes.** Four ship, and their folder names are configuration; all validate through `mode_config.validate_wiki_folder`, which requires the first path segment to be `wiki`, so none escapes that parent. **`generic`'s four keys are `sources_folder`, `entities_folder`, `concepts_folder` and `sessions_folder` — there is no `synthesis` key** (measured 2026-09-07). The cross-source layer is therefore `concepts_folder` set to `wiki/synthesis/`, which validates because the check tests canonical form and the `wiki` root, not the name. Taking the tool's default `wiki/concepts/` instead would work equally well and cost the vault its own word for the layer. `generic` is the structural match — its sources-to-concepts split is this vault's per-source-to-cross-source split. Names confirmed by a round trip through `mode get`:

| Layer                           | Path                                                      | Written by                         |
| ------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- |
| Captured record, one per source | `literatures/<citation key>.md`                           | The capture verb, and nothing else |
| Per-source page                 | `wiki/sources/<title>.md`                                 | The tool                           |
| Cross-source pages              | `wiki/synthesis/<slug>.md`, set through `concepts_folder` | The tool                           |
| Entity and session pages        | `wiki/entities/`, `wiki/sessions/`                        | The tool                           |

`literatures/` stays outside `wiki/`, where the tool provably cannot write: its lint performs no filesystem writes at all — verified byte-identical with `sha256sum` — and its ingest is scope-refused from any path outside `wiki/` and `.raw/`. The vault gives up the `wiki/` parent segment and keeps every name inside it; that is the whole accommodation on the layout axis. (`para` mode names the same four things the vault's `projects/` layer does; whether one tool carries both layers is left to the workflow-component audit.)

**What the tool cannot read, and why that is fine.** Of its seven capture adapters exactly one is implemented, accepting markdown, text, JSON, CSV, YAML and HTML; PDF, EPUB and images are `metadata-only` — identified and described but not extracted — and need an external runner it does not ship. Two consequences bind the seam in §4.4: the compile input is **markdown text, never a path to an attachment**, and the tool's own `capture` command is unused, because its one working input is the file capture already produces. Zotero's index does the extraction, which is work the tool could not do.

**What the vault changes, all of it in non-binding territory.** Three conflicts were measured, each resolved on our side:

1. `wiki/index.md` carries six required frontmatter fields; the vault's `okf-structure` check requires a nested `index.md` to carry none. **Settled in §4.3.1**: ADR 0001 is `current` and OKF §8 is the mechanism it names, so the deviation is recorded and priced rather than waved through.
2. The tool's `capture` treats a folder named `inbox` as its drop zone and, run bare, would copy this vault's fleeting notes into its raw store. Since `capture` is unused, this is avoided by not running it; the plan records the `--inbox` flag and the `.vault-meta/capture/config.json` key as belt and braces.
3. `.raw/captured/*.md` copies carry no frontmatter and would fail `okf-frontmatter`, since verify rglobs everything but `.git`. It does not arise, because nothing writes that store: §4.5 points the ledger at `fulltext/`, and anything `.raw/captured/` held would be **a duplicate of `fulltext/`**, not knowledge. The vault gitignores `.raw/` and `.vault-meta/` and excludes them from the walk on that ground — a duplicate has no place in the bundle even if something later writes one. The tool's `adopt` will not install its own gitignore rules over an existing file without `--force`, so this is the vault's edit to make.

**`adopt`'s write set collides with the vault's own scaffold, and the order resolves it (read 2026-09-07).** `adopt` writes a template tree — `.gitignore`, four files under `.obsidian/`, `.raw/.manifest.json`, `inbox/.gitkeep`, four pages under `wiki/` — plus `.claude-obsidian.json` and both ledgers. Two of those the vault also owns: decomposition decision 17 ships a seeded `.obsidian/` through `scaffold`, and §4.3's conflict list has the vault owning `.gitignore`. **Without `--force`, `adopt` silently skips a file that already exists**, so scaffolding before adopting leaves the vault's versions intact and is the order the plan takes. Adopting first would leave the tool's Obsidian settings in place with no error to notice. `.vault-meta/` is not created by `adopt` at all — it appears on demand and is gitignored.

**Tracers, before any compile task is written.** Each is one command on a scratch vault: that the plugin installs whole and a session with it loaded still completes an ordinary capture run without the context cost forcing a compaction; that `mode set` writes the two folder names and `mode get` reads them back; that a compile run over three captured sources writes only under `wiki/` and leaves `literatures/` byte-identical; and that a page the tool writes passes `verify --surface commit` with the exclusions in place.

**If a tracer fails**, the fallback is to defer compile to its own spec and ship capture, the linter and setup alone — a complete iteration on its own. It is *not* nvk/llm-wiki, which the layout probe found worse on every axis: its interior layout is module constants with no configuration path, and one `--fix` pass moved this vault's root `index.md` into a quarantine folder and took `okf-frontmatter` failures from zero to thirteen. (Its default branch is `master`, so a marketplace entry naming `main` would mislead.)

### 4.3.1 The `wiki/index.md` deviation, and why the vault bends, not the tool

**The tool writes `wiki/index.md` with frontmatter; OKF §8 forbids it on any index file.** The path is hard-coded in three code sites plus `config/capabilities.json`, and the tool's own lint requires six frontmatter keys on every page under `wiki/` — so a frontmatter-free index fails *its* check as surely as a frontmatter-bearing one fails ours. `_ORPHAN_EXCLUDED_NAMES` is orphan-only and does not reach the frontmatter loop; there is no config escape and no environment variable. The finding is exit-0 on `lint` but blocks `checkpoint` outright (`LINT_FAILED`, exit 4), whose only bypass discards dead-link and provenance gating too.

**Resolved by amending ADR 0001 rather than patching the tool (2026-09-07).** A cleaner on a trigger was considered and rejected: it does not avoid the patch — stripping the frontmatter is what *creates* the blocking finding — and it adds a third writer racing a transactional system's hash preconditions. The one-line patch was considered and rejected on the release-model evidence above. What remains is a bounded deviation: one file, in a machine-owned tree, whose frontmatter an OKF consumer ignores under OKF §4.1's producer-key rule. ADR 0001 carries the exemption and `docs/terminology.md` prices it at cost class 1. **The vault's own `structure.py` must gain the matching exemption** — its nested-index rule would otherwise fail the vault on its own check — which is plan work, not a further decision.

### 4.4 The seam between capture and compile

**The captured set is the citable set, and one lint enforces it (adopted 2026-09-07; one addition 2026-09-13 — a note under `literatures/` the reader cannot take, unreadable, not UTF-8, unparseable or tuple-less, is a row from this lint, `UNREACHABLE` for the read fault and `UNMATCHED schema-violation` for the rest, never a silent drop from the set; the lifecycle walk still skips it, which is why this lint carries the row).** A compiled page may cite only a source capture wrote. Nothing enforced that: no check tied a `[[citation key]]` or `[@citation key]` under `wiki/` back to a `literatures/<citation key>.md`, and the adopted tool's own dangling-link check resolves against every file in the vault rather than against the captured set — so a page citing an invented key, or a key belonging to a note the vault never rendered, passes today. That is this project's founding thesis with no mechanism behind it, at the one seam this lane exists to design.

**This is the one place in the lane where building is the answer, and the build bar is met by naming what no candidate can supply**: "captured" is a fact about *this* vault's evidence layer, and nothing external knows what it means. The bar is also low in the way `docs/agents/sourcing.md` cares about — it is a lint over a set the vault already holds, not a subsystem. **The guarantee is structural and the lint guards the one gap it leaves.** A compiled page exists because the wrapper put a source in the ledger, and the wrapper names only sources capture wrote (§4.5), so a page cannot correspond to an uncaptured source by construction. What construction does not cover is **prose**: the tool's LLM writes page bodies, and a body can cite a key nobody captured.

So the lint has two halves. **Structural**: every ledger record's `origin.locator` must name a file capture wrote, which is checkable against the evidence layer and fails loudly if the wrapper is bypassed. **Textual**: any `[[key]]` or `[@key]` under `wiki/` must resolve to **the captured set**, with the remainder reported as a finding. §1.1 records that the captured set is undefined — the keys capture wrote notes for, or the filenames now under `literatures/` — and task 0 settles it before this lint is built, because the two readings differ exactly when a note has been deleted by hand.

**The textual surface is usually empty, and that is the design working rather than the check being vacuous.** Verified at pin `ad67087`: the tool's page vocabulary has no citation-key property — `sources:` holds wikilinks to the tool's *own* page titles, and the source-page fields are `source_type`, `author`, `url`, `source_id`, `sha256`, `authority`, `review_state`, `key_claims`. Nothing it writes by default carries a citation key, so the check fires only when an LLM invents one, which is exactly the failure it exists to catch. Frontmatter values are scanned with the body, because a citation key appearing there would be as invented as one in prose. The tool's own dangling-link check is left alone rather than replaced; it answers a different question, and two checks over one surface is what §6 refused, not two checks over two.

- Capture is the only writer of the literature note and the text cache, and **the text cache is the compile input** — there is no third file. §4.5 names `fulltext/<attachment key>.md` as each source's `origin.locator`, which the tool's `ledgers.py` already accepts as a vault-relative canonical path. No separate compile input file is written into the tool's inbox directory: it would be a second copy of bytes the vault already holds, with a second hash to reconcile. Two constraints bind that input, and both are satisfied by construction rather than by rule: the input's filename is the **attachment key**, so it can never equal `<citation key>.md`, and its frontmatter carries a `fulltext` type with no `title` and no `aliases`, so no wikilink to that source becomes ambiguous across two files. Markdown is not a convenience here, it is the tool's only implemented input format (§4.3).
- Where Zotero has no indexed text, or only a partial index, capture writes no compile input and files a finding. Nothing is compiled from an abstract alone, and nothing is compiled from a page range that Zotero truncated.
- **No second copy of the text exists.** The tool's `.raw/captured/` store would hold one if its own `capture` were run; it is not run (§4.3), and the ledger points at `fulltext/` instead. The gitignore and walk exclusion stay as cheap insurance, guarding a store nothing writes.
- `wiki/synthesis/` is the cross-source layer, and it sits there because **the tool cannot write outside `wiki/`** — its ingest is scope-refused from any other path. **Root `synthesis/` does not survive beside it** (§6): the two would be one epistemic class in two folders, so the layer moves rather than forking. That is the same role `synthesis/` was always intended to serve, so the layer keeps its meaning and its name and moves one level down.
- **The per-source page lives in `wiki/sources/`, not in the literature note.** The vault would have preferred one file per source, but the tool's engine has only whole-file create and replace, so it cannot write part of a note another writer owns. Splicing the page in outside the tool would cost the tool its wikilinks, its index entry and its ledger locator, and its next lint would report both as broken. Two files per source, in two trees, is the price of not editing the tool. This is also why the note's free region retires rather than waiting for a splice that is not coming (§6).
- Compile runs when the person asks, after capture, for one source or a batch. The tool's own gate is the human gate: inspect the proposed bundle, approve it by its plan hash, apply it atomically or not at all. No second gate is added.
- **Capture** records the compile input's sha256 in the note, because §3.2 makes capture the note's sole writer and §3.4's linter only reads and reports — which is now the same `sha256` the provenance tuple already records for the text cache, since they are one file (§3.6). A refreshed note whose hash changed is reported as needing recompile, which the tool then performs.
- Reading across the boundary already works, and **it is how a reader crosses in both directions**: the tool's page carries `[[<citation key>]]`, which resolves into `literatures/` with no dead link and puts that page in the literature note's backlinks. **Reversed 2026-09-07: the literature note embeds the compiled page** (§3.3 step 5). The three objections were to a forward *link*, and none carries to an embed. Capture running first leaves an inert missing-file view rather than a dangling link; `[[<title>]]`'s ambiguity disappears when the target is a path; and the tool renaming its pages is repaired on refresh, because the path comes from the ledger's `pages[]` rather than a guess. What the embed adds over the backlink is that it removes duplication instead of recording a relation — the compiled summary is displayed in the note, never copied into it.

### 4.5 The wrapper: what it owns (2026-09-07)

§4.3 adopts the tool unmodified, so something must drive it, and that something is research-vault's own. Naming a component without bounding it would leave the tier claim unscreenable — `docs/agents/sourcing.md` prices prompt-bearing components at the high bar and glue at the low one, and "glue" spans a shell alias to a subsystem.

**It owns four things.**

1. **Selection** — which captured sources are compiled. The tool cannot decide this: it has no notion of the captured set, which is why §4.4's lint exists at all, and the evidence layer is the vault's.

2. **Locators** — naming `fulltext/<attachment key>.md` as each source's `origin.locator`. The tool's prose says material comes from `inbox/` or `.raw/`, but `ledgers.py` validates only that a locator is a vault-relative canonical path. The wrapper supplies its own locator rather than evading a constraint; the restriction it steps past is instruction, not validation (§4.3).

3. **Ledger records**, at `wiki/meta/ledgers/source-ledger.json` — filling `origin.kind`, `origin.locator`, `title`, `content_kind`, `authority` and `review_status` from the captured metadata. **`fulltext/<attachment key>.md` is confirmed valid as a locator** (read 2026-09-07): the `kind: "file"` branch enforces only canonical vault-relative form and `assert_within`, with no prefix allowlist, so nothing restricts a source to `wiki/`, `inbox/` or `.raw/`. **And no operation type can write there** — `ingest` and `generic` are confined to `wiki/`, `capture` to three-part `.raw/captured/` paths — which is the property the design wants: capture creates the file, the tool only ever reads it.

   **`review_status: "active"` is the demanding value, and the wrapper meets its terms or picks another.** It requires `content_sha256` present *and equal to the file's current bytes*, an observed date (`retrieved_at` or `ingested_at`), an explicit `refresh_due` at or after it, and **every path in `pages[]` to exist**. Since `pages[]` is populated by the compile that has not run yet, a record is written `unreviewed` at capture and moved to `active` only once its pages exist. **The ledger's names are not the page's**: it takes `review_status` and `content_sha256`, while the tool's *page* frontmatter convention spells the same ideas `review_state` and `sha256` and adds `source_id` — documented in `skills/wiki/references/frontmatter.md` and enforced by no code. The record ID is not the wrapper's to choose: it must equal `stable_source_id(kind, locator, content_sha256)`, a truncated digest over the three joined by NULs. — **and note the collision §1.1 asks to be named**: this `authority` is the compile tool's ledger enum, unrelated to the vault field of the same name that §3.2 retires, and setting `content_sha256` to the hash the provenance tuple already records, so the two systems agree by construction rather than by comparison.

4. **Bundle authoring and the two-step apply** — measured 2026-09-07, and it is more than a command call. **There is no `ingest` subcommand**: `ingest` is an `operation_type` inside a hand-authored transaction bundle, and the wrapper composes that JSON itself — `{schema: "claude-obsidian.transaction.v1", operation_id, operation_type: "ingest", expected_hashes, writes[]}`. It then runs `transaction inspect`, reads `approval_sha256` off the result, and runs `transaction apply --approved-plan-sha256 <hash>`; the hash is **mandatory** on apply and a changed bundle raises `PLAN_CHANGED`. `inspect`'s `changed_paths` includes writes the engine expands (the address counter, for one), so the wrapper reports the post-expansion set. Outcome is four-state as everywhere else, with exit 75 meaning re-read and rebuild, and `transaction recover` after an interruption.

**It owns none of these.** Rendering wiki pages, which the tool's own agent does under the tool's own prose. Methodology, which defers to lane 5. Writing under `wiki/` **outside a transaction**. The ledger lives at `wiki/meta/ledgers/source-ledger.json`, inside the tool's tree, so the wrapper does write there — as a `writes` entry in a bundle the engine validates and applies, never as a direct file write. That is the distinction the boundary rests on, not the folder. And lint: §4.4's captured-set check is a vault check on the commit surface beside verify's others, not a wrapper function.

**The tier claim is conditional and it expires.** Bounded this way the wrapper carries no prompts — it selects, fills a record, calls a CLI — so glue is the honest tier today. **Lane 5's methodology work makes it prompt-bearing**, and at that point the tier changes and the component is re-screened rather than grandfathered. Recording that here is what stops a low-bar verdict outliving the shape that earned it.

## 5. Setup and lint integrity (**chosen 2026-09-07**)

Doctor is setup's linter. Each probe below says what it asks and what a failure means, because a probe whose failure is uninterpretable is a check nobody acts on.

| Probe                                             | What it asks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Why it is shaped this way                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One `GET /api/`                                   | reachability, `X-Zotero-Version`, `Zotero-API-Version`, `Zotero-Schema-Version` and `Zotero-Server-ID` in a single round trip                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Four facts arrive in one response's headers; four probes would be four chances to disagree with each other about which instance answered                                                                                                                                                                    |
| **403 is distinguished from every other failure** | whether the local-API preference is off, as against Zotero being absent, asleep, or on another port                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Zotero answers 403 specifically when the preference is disabled. Collapsing it into "unreachable" sends a person to restart an application that is running fine, when the repair is one checkbox                                                                                                            |
| Write-path identity                               | send an id on a write-shaped request and read the status. **At setup there is no recorded id** — no tuple exists yet — so doctor sends a *deliberately wrong* one and requires 412. That proves the guard is armed without echoing the live header back, which §2 names as the implementation that defeats it. Once notes exist, the linter sends the id the tuple recorded | Measured 2026-09-07: the id is checked before any key is presented, so 412 arrives without authorization and without mutating anything. It proves the guard is armed over the path writes actually take, at no cost and with no key (§2). It does not identify the instance — the read probes above do that |
| Full-text availability                            | `extensions.zotero.sync.fulltext.enabled` and `sync.storage.protocol`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | §3.6's layer is derived on the machine that indexed. These two values are why a second machine regenerates rather than receives, and a person who turns sync on has changed that answer                                                                                                                     |
| Better BibTeX presence and version                | `api.ready` over JSON-RPC                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | The citation key is a Better BibTeX artifact; without it capture has no filename (§3.1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Better BibTeX `git` preference                    | the hidden `off` / `config` / `always` value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | It can run git inside an export target, which collides with this repository's explicit-pathspec commit rule. Auto-export retires (§6), so this is a warning about a setting that could still surprise, not a gate                                                                                           |
| Plugin liveness                                   | `active` and `appDisabled` from the running Zotero, **never `strict_max_version` from the packed manifest**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Measured 2026-09-07: four of 23 installed add-ons declare a cap below Zotero 10 and only one is actually disabled. A manifest-derived check gives three false alarms out of four                                                                                                                            |
| Path shim                                         | resolve one `file://` URL through `wslpath`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | An environment necessity, not a choice: Zotero returns Windows paths and the vault runs in WSL (§3.6)                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Translator-format health is a warning, not a failure.** Every translator format returns HTTP 500 on 10.0.1 (§3.7), so a check that fails on it would fail on every healthy machine here. What it usefully reports is a change: the day those formats start answering, a route this design closed has reopened.

**Provisioning the enrichment plugins is part of setup, and carries two obligations.** First, each must be verified to *load*, and the obvious way to check does not work: reading `strict_max_version` from the packed manifests of all 23 installed add-ons finds four capping below Zotero 10 — `KeepZotero` 7.0, `Scite` 8.0, `Zotero TLDR` 7.0, `DOI Manager` 9.0 — but Zotero has disabled only the last. For the other three `extensions.json` records `targetApplications` `6.999` to `*` with `appDisabled` false, and two are running, so a manifest-derived check raises **three false alarms out of four**. `appDisabled` read with `active` on the running Zotero is the answering field (decomposition §6.1). A plugin the vault relies on that Zotero refuses to load is a check that silently never runs. Second, those that can run automatically must be switched on: the DOI manager hooks the item-add notifier behind `extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve` and sweeps monthly, the PMCID fetcher fires on the same notifier behind `extensions.zotero.pmcid.auto`. Without those preferences the work waits for a click, and an item the agent adds (§2) is never enriched. Installing is a human wizard step; doctor reports presence, loadability and whether automatic mode is on.

The server id has one authoritative home: the running Zotero, which returns it on every response. Doctor reports it and the tuple records what was live at capture; nothing caches a third copy. The linter never compares two stored copies — it sends the recorded id as a header and lets Zotero answer 412 (§3.4). That costs nothing and needs no key, because the id check precedes authentication (§2): doctor confirms the database over the *write* path without holding a write key and without mutating anything.

The standing facts file is deleted rather than repaired. `docs/environment.md` recorded Zotero 9.0.6 and Better BibTeX 9.0.57 while 10.0.1 and 9.0.63 were running, while claiming to be live-verified throughout. Thirteen of its eighteen rows were probe-answerable, measured on a stack that has moved, or about the retired auto-export. The other five were not environment facts: two rationale, one an interpreter policy belonging in `pyproject.toml`, two Better BibTeX preference readings no probe can reach — all now recorded where they are used, in §3.6 and §9. Live values come from `probe`, and nothing keeps a second copy to go stale. That removes the failure class rather than watching for it, the same reasoning §6 applies to the DOI and metadata checks.

Retired from setup: the whole-library auto-export step and its staleness probe. Kept: the human-only wizard steps for Zotero plugin installs.

Every mechanical step in this spec is falsifiable, and for most the instrument is a linter (invariant 6 makes a linter one instrument, not the requirement): setup has doctor; capture, the CSL file, and the text cache have the lifecycle linter through recorded hashes and versions; adding has capture, which runs immediately on the returned keys and reports what it could not read.

## 6. What changes in the existing machinery

Retired by this spec (**confirmed 2026-09-07**):

- the Better BibTeX auto-export contract: observation, byte-compare, the `staleness` verb, the `autoexport` doctor probe, and the `bibliography.py` machinery around them.

  **This is the one retirement where the spec and shipped code disagree.** Measured 2026-09-07, the contract is live and load-bearing: `scaffold.py:383` has doctor call `observe_autoexport`; `autoexport` sits in **both** `DOCTOR_HARD_UNMATCHED` and `DOCTOR_HARD_UNREACHABLE` (`__main__.py:45-46`), so a persistent mismatch is a hard failure today; `staleness` is a shipped CLI verb; and `bibliography.py` is 763 lines. Retiring means deleting a doctor probe that currently hard-fails, a verb, and most of a module.

  **What makes it right is that the artifact is now obtainable on demand.** §3.3 step 4 regenerates the whole CSL file from one 0.46-second read, so the observe-and-wait machinery guards something we can simply produce. Better BibTeX's documentation also confirms the retirement's premise exactly: `//` is *"a library-name prefix on a collection path"*, not library scope, so `autoexport.add("//")` was never going to register a whole-library export and no RPC route exists. The human-repair text at `bibliography.py:598` — *"a person must create or fix the whole-library Better CSL JSON auto-export in BBT Preferences"* — retires with it, which removes the design's last instruction-to-a-human rung.

- `research_vault/zotero.py`'s hardcoded `base: str = "http://localhost:23119"`. Two Zotero instances now run on this machine at once, distinguished only by port (§9), so the base URL is configuration, not a constant. The default may stay; being unable to name another instance is what retires.

- the note-level screening status, `lint_screening_state`, and the `superseded-note` reason code;

- claim lines as an ingest output, and anchors derived from annotations.

Replaced rather than superseded: ADR 0004 and 0005 were suspended for re-derivation here and **were deleted on 2026-09-07** once this spec supplied their successors, so there is nothing left to negotiate with. ADR 0001, 0002 and 0003 bind (see the header). It states the two positions directly, and whether either becomes a record again is the register's business: identity is the Zotero item key qualified by the server id, and the citation key is the name; capture is the sole writer of the vault's bibliographic record. The text cache is a new derived artifact outside the git boundary, the same class of thing as Zotero's own attachment store. The glossary is rewritten per §1.1, as a fresh derivation, not an amendment. The supersession of foundation spec §4 is stated once, in the header.

Kept, each with the reason it survives. Existing code is not a reason (see the header), so anything that could not give one went back on the table, and three did.

| Kept                                     | Why it is not adopted or dropped                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Four-state results                       | A reporting discipline, not a component: its whole content is that an outage must never read as a pass and a skipped check must never read as clean. Nothing is available to adopt because nothing external knows what this vault means by a check.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Deprecate-never-delete for vault records | Same class. The record's value depends on negative knowledge staying inspectable, which is a rule about writing, not a mechanism.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| The path shim                            | An environment necessity on this machine, not a choice: Zotero returns Windows paths and the vault runs in WSL.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Findings and the review queue            | Screened against the adopted tool, and it does not cover the ground. The tool's lint engine writes no files at all by design, so a durable non-blocking finding has nowhere to live in it; its only reaction is to refuse before writing state, which is the opposite of the disposition §3.4 chose. Its source ledger addresses vault-relative paths and HTTPS URLs, so a Zotero item key, version, trash membership or server id cannot be named in it, and six of the seven lifecycle reason codes are inexpressible. It has no acknowledgment concept carrying an actor, a date, a reason and a content hash. Two surfaces would mean neither gets drained, so the vault keeps one and does not add checks the tool already runs under `wiki/`. |
| verify's update-notice check             | Screened, and it stays. Zotero detects retractions natively, in partnership with Retraction Watch, and warns at cite time. It exposes that verdict nowhere a client can read: no endpoint, no field in item JSON or `meta`, the flag living in a local SQLite table. So the work is duplicated for the person and unavailable to the vault. Ours stays until upstream exposes it, and asking them to is worth an issue.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

Retired here, on screening rather than on scope:

- **The managed region and the free region below it.** No writer survives. The per-source summary moved to the tool's tree (§4.4), claim lines retire above, and a hook already deny-lists agents from `literatures/` entirely, so no skill could reach the free region even today. No literature note on this machine has ever carried prose there. Against that sat roughly 120 lines of boundary machinery across `notes.py`, `lints.py` and `claims.py`, plus 49 test references across 13 files, whose only purpose was keeping two owners apart in one file. Per-source human prose gets a better home than an unwritten region: a Zotero child note, which capture already reads and now renders (§3.3 step 5). `managed-sha256` narrows to a hash over the body below the frontmatter, and capture writes it into the frontmatter alongside the tuple (§3.2).
- **verify's `doi` and `metadata` checks.** Screened against the enrichment catalogue in the sourcing note and adopted away. Installable Zotero plugins verify and clean DOIs, retrieve missing ones from Crossref, DBLP, Semantic Scholar and arXiv, resolve PMID and PMCID, and refresh metadata in bulk. They fix the record in the library, so capture's snapshot inherits the correction and the check leaves the vault. The signal survives the move: these plugins report a failure by tagging the item, and §3.2's snapshot carries `tags` for that reason, so an unverifiable DOI is still visible here without the vault making the call itself. This is the design's own premise applied to itself: Zotero is the source of truth, and a check that duplicates what a Zotero plugin does in Zotero is work in the wrong place. Which plugins the vault asks for is a setup step (§5), not code.

Recategorised: `find-sources` is deferred to the search spec, not kept on merit, and the `not-admitted` reason code stays in service because that skill still files it.

Reason codes joining the registry: `re-keyed`, `merged`, `trashed`, `deleted`, `database-changed`. **`docs/terminology.md` §4.4 governs how**: kebab-case, and the `REASON_CODES` row updated *in the same change* that adds the identifier, kept on one physical table row for the parity checks. All five are already kebab-case. `drift` and `outage` already exist. `superseded-note` leaves with the note status. Three producers named elsewhere in this spec still owe a code and the plan assigns them: the propagation residue check (§3.5), capture's no-text and partial-index case (§4.4), and the recompile-needed report (§4.4).

Frozen, dispositioned in the workflow-component audit — **seven, and this is the list**: the quote check, `lint_evidence_layer`, factcheck, `lint_claim_immutability`, stance links, the publish gate, and project-flow. A later paragraph calls four of them out by their shared cause; it is naming a subset, not a different list. **`archive-source` is not among them**: §2 retires `archive.py`, and a verb cannot be frozen when its only handler is deleted (`__main__.py:351`), so the verb goes with the module.

**The offline commit contract is amended, and that is a real cost.** `research_vault/templates/git/pre-commit` opens by calling itself offline and runs verify with `--offline`. Invariant 5 would put the lifecycle linter at pre-commit, and that linter needs a running Zotero, so either the hook becomes online for this one check or the linter runs at capture and verify only. **Invariant 5 holds that leg**, so this iteration takes the second and the offline contract is not amended after all. The case for the first is recorded because the held leg will be revisited: a commit is the last moment before a stale projection becomes history, and the check would report UNREACHABLE without blocking when Zotero is closed, which §3.4 already specifies.

**The synthesis layer moves under `wiki/`, and the reason is epistemic before it is mechanical.** The tool cannot write outside `wiki/`, which settles where its output goes — but the move would be right without that constraint. **Nothing under `wiki/` passes an evidence gate or follows the evidence rules**: the compiled pages are ungated LLM output asserting arrangement, and `wiki/` is the folder that says so at a glance. `CONTEXT.md` already gives the synthesis layer exactly that status — *"freely rewritable because it asserts arrangement, not evidence"* — so root `synthesis/` and `wiki/synthesis/` would be two folders for one epistemic class, which is the duplication this spec keeps removing. The layer keeps its meaning and its name and moves one level down.

**What that costs, stated.** Root `synthesis/` leaves `scaffold.VAULT_DIRS` and `structure`'s folder-to-type map, so `check_tree` stops attesting it. **No type is derived under `wiki/`, and that is a decision, not an omission**: the tool writes `type: source`, `concept`, `entity` and `meta` on its pages, so adding `wiki/synthesis → synthesis` to `_FOLDER_TYPES` would fail `check_note_frontmatter` on every page it writes. §9 records why the pages pass today — both sides demand a non-empty `type`, neither derives a value for `wiki/`. the Bases view and templates repoint. `CONTEXT.md`'s **Synthesis layer**, **Synthesis note** and **Type (OKF)** definitions — the last derives the type from `synthesis/`, so leaving it is how the folder-to-type question survives into code name the old path and are rewritten in task 0 (§1.1). The evidence layer stays where it is: `literatures/` is gated, and keeping it outside `wiki/` is the same boundary drawn from the other side. Today `synthesis-conventions` tells an agent to compose pages by hand carrying `[[citation key#^claim-id]]` claim links; under this spec the adopted compile step maintains them and claim links are no longer the arrangement's currency. That skill is therefore rewritten, not frozen, and its 2+-source threshold and index-registration rules are checked against whatever the adopted tool enforces so the vault does not carry two conflicting sets of rules for one folder.

Stance links remain frozen pending the workflow-component audit. They live on synthesis pages, so the audit decides whether the compiled pages carry them or whether disputes are recorded the adopted tool's way.

The same reservation covers every frozen check that reads what this spec stops producing. Retiring claim lines removes the only producer the quote check, the evidence-layer check, and factcheck consume, and it leaves `lint_claim_immutability` guarding a syntax nothing writes. Those four are frozen rather than retired, and none of them is removed until the audit says so, so the retirement of claim lines is a retirement of the ingest-side producer only, not of the checks downstream of it.

Rewritten skills: `import-source`, `setup-vault` and `synthesis-conventions`. **`synthesis-conventions` is rewritten twice over**: its claim-link currency retires with claim lines, and it reads and registers into `synthesis/index.md`, a path that moves under `wiki/` (§6). **`setup-vault`'s rewrite has a pinned test in its way** — `tests/test_skill_files.py:94-96` asserts three exact strings from its line 44 — so skill and test change in one commit. All three names are governed by `docs/terminology.md` §4.3, which renames them outright if task 0's vocabulary reaches them. Others untouched until their own spec.

## 7. Testing (**chosen 2026-09-07**)

**What exists** (source read 2026-09-05). `tests/conftest.py` builds the `tmp_vault`, `fixture_vault` and `net_vault` fixtures and gates the live suite in `pytest_collection_modifyitems`, skipping the `live` marker without `RV_LIVE=1` and `live_net` without `RV_LIVE_NET=1`. Seven marked live tests run under that gate across `test_cli_live.py`, `test_zotero.py`, `test_scaffold_live.py`, `test_archive.py` and `test_verify_cli.py`. Every network response in the 24,571-line suite is hand-written and injected with `monkeypatch` — `FakeTransport` cans the Better BibTeX JSON-RPC payloads, `_fake_network` the HTTP ones. Nothing is recorded from a live service; the `*.manifest.json` files are form-owner manifests, not responses.

**Offline suite, on fixtures in the style the suite already uses.** The payloads are small: the versions maps are flat `{"KEY": version}`, `/items/<key>/fulltext` is `{content, indexedPages, totalPages}` or `{content, indexedChars, totalChars}`, and the third read is trimmed to `key`, `citationKey` and `relations` per item (§9). All six transitions are now observed rather than composed — five from ordinary reads (drifted, re-keyed, merged via `dc:replaces`, trashed, database changed) and **deleted** from the sitting below. *Database changed* needs no bogus header: with two instances running, pointing a request at the other port is the real condition, not a stand-in for it.

**No recorder.** The bulky item JSON *is* needed — §3.4 step 3 reads `/items/top?format=json` for `citationKey` and `relations.dc:replaces`, and the re-keyed and merged fixtures come from nowhere else — but one item's JSON hand-trimmed to those two fields is a small fixture, not a recording. If the plan wants record-and-replay, `pytest-recording` or `vcrpy` in the dev extra does it with redaction hooks; neither is a dependency today. A recorder verb inside `research_vault` is the expensive option — it puts a production surface under the coverage, mutation and form gates permanently.

**The substrate.** A second Zotero runs from `~/.zotero/zotero/rfnse7tz.default` against `~/Zotero/`, unsynced, on port 23129 while production keeps 23119; the two report different server ids and answer at once. Better BibTeX 9.0.63 is installed with the library's citekey pattern, giving 1,455 keyed bibliographic items for the capture leg to bind to. **It runs one add-on where production runs 23, by decision.** The enrichment plugins write on item-add and on scheduled sweeps — the DOI manager behind `extensions.shortdoi.autoretrieve`, the PMCID fetcher behind `extensions.zotero.pmcid.auto`, the attachment scanner by tagging — and every measurement here is a before-and-after read. A plugin editing items in the background would not make the substrate more realistic; it would make the readings unattributable. Lane 2 screens the plugins and declares them in the README table a human wizard step installs from (decomposition decision 17); one earns a place here when that screen gives a reason to exercise it.

**What the sitting established (2026-09-07).** Authorize returned a remembered key. A human edit moved item `ALKT2NF7` from version 0 to 1708. A re-index of attachment `568BWIT7` moved no version at all — and provably ran, because `.zotero-ft-cache` was rewritten while the PDF was untouched — confirming §3.6 by observation. A Path A create returned an item with `citationKey: null`, keyed by Better BibTeX six seconds later (§2). A trash-then-permanent-delete produced the **deleted** fixture. Snapshots and the log live outside the repository at `~/zotero-test-sitting/findings/`: 130 KB of machine-specific maps whose value is the conclusions drawn here. Only the authorize dialog needs a person, so the legs repeat unattended.

**What the deleted fixture shows, and one thing it does not.** A trashed item is absent from `/items?format=versions` while `GET /items/<key>` still answers 200; permanent deletion then removes it from `items/trash?format=versions`. *Trashed* and *deleted* are therefore indistinguishable in the items map alone, which is why the linter reads both. **But the recorded pair cannot replay the trashed transition.** No snapshot holds the scratch item while it was live — it was created after the baseline and trashed before the next capture — so the pair shows a key *appearing* in the trash map having never been in the items map, rather than *leaving* one as it *enters* the other. That signature comes from a live probe, not the fixture; only *deleted* replays correctly. **The plan's testing task takes one more snapshot between create and trash**, which closes it; until then the trashed fixture is marked in the fixture set as non-replayable.

**Live legs** against Zotero 10 on a scratch collection, under the existing `live` marker: capture round-trip, and linter classification after a live edit.

**`verify --as-of YYYY-MM-DD`, defaulting to today.** A *stamp* records when something happened and reads one clock; a *check* decides whether something is stale and must take the instant as an argument, or its verdict is not reproducible. Three surfaces compare dates — the update-notice check, the inbox's `oldest_age_days`, and the ledger's `refresh_due`. **The vault does the third comparison, because nothing else does** (read 2026-09-07): `ledgers.py` defines `source_is_stale(record, as_of=)` but its only callers gate whether a source counts as standalone evidence for a claim; the lint engine never calls it, so a record past its `refresh_due` produces no finding from the tool. The vault therefore reports it — **using the tool's own predicate rather than a bare `refresh_due < as_of`**, so the two can never disagree about what stale means: `due is None or observed is None or observed > as_of or due < as_of` — and threading one argument to them replaces four hidden `datetime.now()` call sites (`__main__.py:268` and `:613`, `searchlog.py:80`, `inbox.py:342`). **This is what makes the fixtures above replayable**: without it a recorded fixture's verdict drifts with the clock, and a note that verified `current` in September fails in December for no reason present in the fixture. It is also OKF §5.5's own reasoning from the consumer side — OKF fixes an absolute instant on the producer so the reader's `now` is the only variable left, and `--as-of` makes that variable explicit.

**Gates unchanged**: coverage, mutation baseline, form owners.

## 8. Open points

Each is either a decision a later lane owns, or a task the plan carries. Nothing here blocks the plan being written; item 12 blocks one task set inside it, and the plan settles it first.

04. Verb and file names, after the glossary is fixed, including the CSL file's own name.

05. **The BibLaTeX projection.** foundation spec §4 ruled that when LaTeX drafting starts, the BibLaTeX file is a research-vault-regenerated projection over tier 2 — `bibliography.json` filtered by `literatures/` membership, converted CSL→BibLaTeX — of the `log.md` artifact class, machine-projected and regenerated whole. Better BibTeX stays sole writer of the one universe file and no second auto-export exists. **Out of scope here**: it belongs to the `long-form` step, which the decomposition's §15.11 records as having no lane and no issue. Recorded so the ruling survives the step's homelessness.

06. **The presence marker.** foundation spec §4 allowed exactly one write-back, a MarkDB-Connect `has-vault-note` tag, and banned content write-back. The obligation — making vault presence visible in Zotero — is answered "nothing for now" by the decomposition's §15.16, and §3.8 finds MarkDB-Connect writes one tag and no files, so it composes with this design rather than competing. **It is also armed destructively on this machine and must not be run before it is fixed** (decomposition §15.16, measured 2026-09-05): `mdbc.removetags` is unset and defaults to `keepsynced`, the branch that *removes* tags for notes it cannot find; `sourcedir` points at a path whose parent does not exist; and `yamlkeyword` is `citekey` where this spec standardises on `citationKey`. A run today enumerates zero notes and strips the live tags — issue #31's data-loss mode. **Deferred, with an owner.**

07. ~~The acknowledgment scope has no hash.~~ **Closed 2026-09-07: stop choosing a hash and derive the identity.** *Built in Part A Task 18 (2026-09-13), with two refinements the build forced: every scope leg strips verify's own `[failed-verification::]` marker bytes before hashing, and the note canonicaliser excludes the verifier-owned `failed-verification` event list as it excludes `verified` events — otherwise an acknowledgment was orphaned by its own clear, or oscillated fail → pass → fail on a note without a `managed-sha256`.* An acknowledgment is identified by `sha256(check_id \0 target \0 target_content_hash)`, so a changed target yields a different id, the ack no longer matches, and the finding re-fires — which is `CONTEXT.md`'s rule with no comparison logic to get wrong. For a note-targeted finding the third component is `managed-sha256`, which §3.2 already writes. **It also closes the decomposition's §15.22**: a doctor row has no path or hash today, but `target` takes a probe id and the third component takes whatever the probe observed, so one identity shape covers note findings and doctor rows. `__main__.py:581-648` already plumbs `target_hash`; this replaces passing a hash with deriving one. The pattern is `stable_source_id`'s, and OKF §5.1 gives the argument for it in its own case — keys are stable rather than positional *"because agents constantly rewrite these documents"*.

08. *Plan-level, not a design decision (2026-09-07).* **`[retraction-ack:: <code>]` was defined once, for three consumers.** The syntax survives in `skills/evidence-conventions`, but §5's rule — that the update-notice gate, `publish` and `evidence-conventions` **all key on that single syntax** — does not. The single-definition-site rule is what keeps three consumers from drifting apart; nothing now states it. ~~**Open**, and cheap to close by naming the definition site.~~ **Closed 2026-09-13 (Part A Task 18):** `publish.RETRACTION_ACK_FIELD` is the one definition site, the skill's text is asserted against it by test, and the three consumers read the constant.

09. *Plan-level, not a design decision (2026-09-07).* **A human acknowledgment does not clear `[failed-verification::]`, though §5 says it should.** Measured: `_mutate_marker` has exactly two callers — `verify.py:834` stamps on UNMATCHED, `:836` clears on MATCHED. The ack path (`cmd_ack` → `inbox.append_ack`) never reaches it. This is an **unimplemented rule, not a lost one**: deleting §5 silently would have hidden a live gap between a written contract and the code. **Implemented 2026-09-13 (Part A Task 18, three rounds):** `ack` clears the marker where verify writes it — every project note carrying the anchor for an anchored target, every project line citing the key for verify's own bare-key `citation-key` findings, the named file for a repo-path target, hand-authored literature notes included and `wiki/` skipped as the writer skips it — and the stamp itself now runs only over outcomes no acknowledgment covers, so the clear is not undone by the next run. The marker's meaning became: a raw failure no person has acknowledged, closed and reopened together with its inbox row.

10. **Web-Annotation context capture is unowned by the document that specifies capture.** foundation spec §5 required quote claims to capture `exact` plus 32-character `prefix`/`suffix` at extraction time, on the reasoning that it is *"nearly free at capture, unreconstructable later"*. This document has zero occurrences of `selector`, `prefix`, `suffix` or `TextQuoteSelector`. The cost asymmetry is the whole argument and it is real — if capture does not take the context, no later pass can. **Open**, and it interacts with the annotation deferral: annotations defer to lane 5 (§3.2), so the anchor whose durability §5 guaranteed is deferred with them.

11. **The re-anchoring cascade lost its trigger.** foundation spec §5 deferred it *"gated on a consistency check, only if the slice shows prose-parsing bottlenecks."* The surviving register entry carries the deferral without the condition, so nothing says when to revisit it. **Open** — a deferral with no trigger is indistinguishable from an abandonment.

12. ~~The rename log has no site.~~ **Closed 2026-09-07: the applied plan is the log.** Propagation computes a plan, prints it, and applies only against that plan's hash — the shape §3.5's blast-radius argument has always implied and the one mutation in this design that touches human content. The applied plan already carries the old→new mapping, every surface it rewrote, and one hash, so a second artifact would be a copy of it. Capture takes no such gate and needs none: it is deterministic, idempotent by render-compare, and writes only machine-owned trees, so an approval step would guard nothing.
