OKF does not reach this — §1 puts runtime out of scope — but §10's attestation is the same shape, sanctioning a computation and comparing what ran against it, and §10.3 gives the reason: a fixed surface is what makes *"did the sanctioned thing run"* a mechanical comparison rather than a judgement call.

13. **Export configuration is pinnable, even though the version is not.** BBT supports `preferencesOverride` and `postscriptOverride`: a `preferences.json` or `postscript.js` placed **in the export directory** overrides the global preference for that export alone — committable, reviewable, and immune to another project's global setting. The decomposition's **decision 12** says Zotero-side components are declared and observed, never pinned; that is right about the *version* and too pessimistic about export *behaviour*. **Not taken now, because the auto-export retirement removes the export directory it would live in.** Recorded so that if a configured export ever returns, the pin is known to exist rather than rediscovered.

14. **A deprecated `Extra` syntax exists that our snapshot would not make legible.** BBT still honours `{:pmid: 123456}` alongside `PMID: 123456`, describing it as *"supported but considered deprecated"* and still showing it in its docs. BBT destructures both into CSL; §3.2 copies `Extra` verbatim and parses neither, so an item in the brace form would carry a value in `bibliography.json` and an opaque string in the note. **Measured 2026-09-07: zero of 440 items with a non-empty `Extra` use the brace form**, so this is a watch-item, not a case to build for.

15. **Two pull-export aliases in BBT's own documentation are wrong on 9.0.63.** `pull.md` lists `csljson`, `yaml`, `yml` and `cslyaml` as valid `format` values; measured, several return HTTP 500. The working spelling is `json`. Recorded because `csljson` is the intuitive choice and the documentation invites it — and because §3.3 step 4's route is undocumented, so its neighbours' documentation is the only guide a later reader has.

16. **BBT's export worker is self-described as experimental and self-disabling.** *"a wild departure from how translation works in Zotero"*, and a single error silently flips the whole instance to foreground exports until a human intervenes. Every pull-export request defaults to the worker path. Not a blocker — but if capture's CSL read becomes slow rather than failing, this is the first thing to check.

This is why §3.6 ignores the field                                                                                                                                                                                                   | live probe and SQLite read of a copy, instance stopped, 2026-09-07                  |
| Full-text corpus fetch cost                                       | 19 ms per attachment over the local API, so all 1,367 listed attachments are about 26 s and 97 MB. A full re-read is cheap enough that §3.6 needs no incremental mechanism                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | live probe 2026-09-07, 50-attachment sample extrapolated                            |
| Write guard chain                                                 | No `Zotero-Server-ID` returns **428**; the wrong id (the other instance's, or garbage) returns **412** `does not match this server`; the correct id returns **401** `API key required`. A wrong id is refused **before any key is presented**, so §5's probe costs nothing. The codes do not establish an evaluation order, and nothing rests on one. Confirmed on PATCH, POST and DELETE                                                                                                                                                                                   | live probe 2026-09-07, no-op body and stale precondition so nothing mutated         |
| `/deleted`, second instance                                       | 404 on the test instance as well, so the absence is a property of the local API rather than of one database's state                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | live probe 2026-09-07                                                               |
| Test profile add-ons                                              | Empty at first. Better BibTeX 9.0.63 was then installed from the production profile's own XPI and its citekey pattern set to match; the instance reports 1,455 keyed bibliographic items                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | installed and verified 2026-09-07                                                   |
| Test and production profile divergence                            | Aligned deliberately: Better BibTeX 9.0.63 on both, and the test profile's `citekeyFormat` set to the library's `authEtal2.lower.len + year`. Better BibTeX had written `fillKeyAfter` 1 there against its own default of 2, which production leaves unset; the override was removed. What still differs is 22 further add-ons on production and none here                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `prefs.js` read and aligned 2026-09-07                                              |
| Citation keys are stored, not derived                             | A copy of this library in a fresh profile, with an empty Better BibTeX key store and a **different** citekey pattern (BBT's default against the library's `authEtal2.lower.len + year`), returned all 1,452 shared keys unchanged, disambiguation suffixes included. Setting the pattern to the library's changed none of them either. Two patterns, both directions, zero re-keys                                                                                                                                                                                          | live probe 2026-09-07                                                               |
| A version-0 object moves off 0                                    | Measured twice on the test instance. Through Better BibTeX's RPC: an item at version 0 took version **1695**. Through **a human edit in the Zotero interface** (2026-09-07 sitting): item `ALKT2NF7` moved from version 0 to **1708**. The design's actual path is the second, and it is now exercised rather than inferred                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | live mutation and attended sitting 2026-09-07                                       |
| A regenerate that returns the same key does not touch the version | `item.regenerate_key` returned the existing key on two items and left both versions untouched (13, and 1695 on a second call against the item just re-keyed). Whether Better BibTeX recomputed to the same answer or short-circuited an unchanged item is indistinguishable from outside. Either way §3.5's reason for never using it as a detector holds: it cannot report what *would* change without changing it                                                                                                                                                         | live mutation on the test instance 2026-09-07                                       |
| Manifest ranges do not predict loadability                        | Four of the 23 installed add-ons declare `strict_max_version` below Zotero 10 (KeepZotero 7.0, Scite 8.0, Zotero TLDR 7.0, DOI Manager 9.0), but only DOI Manager is `appDisabled`; `extensions.json` records the other three as `targetApplications` 6.999 to `*`. A manifest-derived check gives three false alarms out of four. `appDisabled` on the running Zotero is the answering field                                                                                                                                                                               | XPI manifest and `extensions.json` read 2026-09-07                                  |
| Add-on inventory                                                  | 23 installed: 13 active, 9 user-disabled, 1 `appDisabled` (`zoteroshortdoi@wiernik.org` 1.6.0)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `extensions.json` read 2026-09-07                                                   |
| The auto-export is live                                           | Production preferences carry an enabled Better BibTeX auto-export to a `bibliography.json` under the repository's former name, `type` library, status `done`. §6 retires this                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `prefs.js` read 2026-09-07                                                          |
| Re-index moves no version                                         | Attachment `568BWIT7` re-indexed from the interface during the 2026-09-07 sitting. The re-index provably ran — `.zotero-ft-cache` was rewritten at 12:14:50 while the PDF itself was untouched since June — and the full-text version stayed **0**, the content sha256 and its 33,822 bytes identical. §3.6 confirmed by observing a re-index rather than by inferring from the all-zeros table                                                                                                                                                                             | attended sitting 2026-09-07                                                         |
| Extracted text is not portable                                    | The same attachment yields 34,024 bytes on production and 33,822 on the test instance, with different hashes. Two machines indexing one PDF do not produce identical text, so a recorded `sha256` is machine-local                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | live probe 2026-09-07                                                               |
| Trash empties itself                                              | `extensions.zotero.trashAutoEmptyDays` defaults to 30 and is unset here. The trash held 504 keys at 11:47 and 212 at 12:02, and a database copy from 11:42 still held 487 `deletedItems` rows, so about 280 items left during a restart this session with no user action. Deletion is not always a deliberate act (§3.5)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | live probe and SQLite read of the pre-session backup, 2026-09-07                    |
| A trashed item leaves the items map                               | While trashed, `II7E6CVR` was absent from `/items?format=versions` and present in `items/trash?format=versions`, and `GET /items/<key>` still answered 200. Trashed and deleted are indistinguishable in the items map alone                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | attended sitting 2026-09-07                                                         |
| The deleted transition, recorded                                  | Trash returned 204 and permanent delete returned 204; the key left the trash map and the item stopped resolving. §7's one unreachable fixture is now observed rather than hand-edited                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | attended sitting 2026-09-07                                                         |
| Path A, end to end                                                | **Always Allow** returned a 32-character key with `remember: true`, persisted to the profile's `localAPIKeys.json` under the caller's `appName`. One key then carried `POST /collections`, `POST /items`, a trashing `PATCH` and a `DELETE` with no re-authorization                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | attended sitting 2026-09-07                                                         |
| A new item is keyed, but not in the response                      | The item created over the local API came back with `citationKey: null`; Better BibTeX had filled `sitting2026` when it was re-read six seconds later, consistent with its `fillKeyAfter` delay of 2 seconds. Capture must re-read rather than trust the create response                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | attended sitting 2026-09-07                                                         |
| Page `type` is required but never checked                         | The compile tool's lint requires six frontmatter keys on every page under `wiki/` and records key *names* only, never validating a value; `structure.py` requires a non-empty `type` and derives none for `wiki/`. Both demand presence, neither derives a value, so the tool's pages pass as written. It tolerates an empty `type:`, which ours rejects                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | source read 2026-09-07                                                              |
| Preprint-to-published is a Zotero merge                           | `zotero-arxiv-workflow` 0.3.9 calls `Zotero.Items.merge(preprintItem, [publishedItem])` — the **preprint is the master**. Its `merge.reservedKeys` default preserves the preprint's key, tags, collections and relations while the published metadata overwrites the rest, and `setType()` flips the item type. Zotero's merge then writes `dc:replaces`. Identity survives, so the vault sees `drift`                                                                                                                                                                      | XPI source read 2026-09-07                                                          |

## Appendix A — Terminology evidence (research note of 2026-09-04)

Absorbed 2026-09-13 from `docs/research/2026-09-04-import-terminology.md`, whose only consumer was this spec: it is the evidence base §1.1's terms were chosen from, kept with the design so the trace survives, and it **binds nothing and describes no current state** — the working labels it records (import, admission, digest, citekey, screening state) are the ones §1.1 retired. Its own text follows unchanged; headings are demoted one level.

Research note, 2026-09-04. Industry-terminology evidence for the import redesign glossary.

**What this is.** A survey of the terms four bodies of practice use for eight concepts the vault needs to name during the import redesign. Every row quotes a primary source verbatim and records where the quote sits, which of the eight concepts it touches, and what the term means elsewhere.

**What consumes it.** The 2026-09-04 import brainstorm and the import redesign spec. The glossary in `CONTEXT.md` currently defines Admission, Import, Screening state, Citekey and Item with working labels; those working labels are reproduced below as given, not as adopted terms.

**The concepts.** The vault is a private git repository of markdown notes fed from a local Zotero library; an LLM agent operates it.

- K1: The whole process of getting a source from outside the vault into the vault (working label: import).
- K2: The human decision that a source is accepted into the library (working label: admission).
- K3: The act of creating the library record in Zotero, whether by a human or by an agent on the human's instruction (working label: admission / add / save).
- K4: The deterministic copy of a record's metadata, attachments, annotations, and derived text into the vault, with provenance (working label: capture).
- K5: The LLM step that turns a captured source into wiki pages: summary page, concept-page updates (working label: digest).
- K6: Detecting that the library-side record changed, vanished, or was re-keyed after capture, and refreshing the vault copy (working labels: drift check, refresh, sync).
- K7: The stable identifier the vault uses for a source across its life (working labels: citekey, item key, identity).
- K8: A source that was accepted but later excluded, superseded, or removed, with the record kept (working labels: screening state, tombstone).

**Nothing here is a decision.** The tables carry no ranking and no recommendation. A working label appearing in a collision column records where a field already uses that word; it does not settle whether the vault keeps the label.

**Conventions.** The verbatim definition column reproduces source text unchanged, including its punctuation. Authored text (the standardness note column, the collisions column, the collisions and gaps sections) is the note's own wording. Where a source uses a vertical bar inside a quote, it is escaped for the table. Field labels name the body of practice and, in parentheses, the source family.

______________________________________________________________________

### Method

Four vocabularies were searched. Each subsection lists every search and page read, with the terms sought and the number of hits, including reads that returned nothing. A verification pass re-read a sample of rows against the cited page; no row was refuted in any vocabulary, so no row in the concept tables carries the refuted marker.

### Concept tables

Columns: term, field, verbatim definition, source and where, collisions, standardness note. Rows are grouped by concept and, within a concept, by vocabulary in the order of the method section. No row was refuted in verification, so none carries the refuted marker.

#### K1: the whole process of getting a source from outside the vault into the vault

Working label: import.

| Term                                                                                  | Field                                              | Verbatim definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Source and where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Collisions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Standardness note                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Ingest Functional Entity                                                              | digital preservation (OAIS)                        | Ingest Functional Entity: The OAIS functional entity that provides the services and functions to accept Submission Information Packages from Producers (or from internal elements under Administration control), including quality assurance and prepare Archival Information Packages for storage, and ensures that Archival Information Packages and their supporting Descriptive Information become established within the OAIS.                     | CCSDS 650.0-M-3, Reference Model for an Open Archival Information System (OAIS), Recommended Practice, December 2024 (= ISO 14721), https://public.ccsds.org/Pubs/650x0m3.pdf; §1.6.2 Terminology, pp. 1-8 to 1-15 (PDF pp. 19-27)                                                                                                                                                         | K4: OAIS Ingest spans acceptance of the SIP through construction of the AIP, so it covers both K1 and K4. K6: the SIP definition covers 'update' of an existing AIP.                                                                            | The field-wide term for the whole intake process, carried by an ISO standard. M-2 (June 2012, now marked 'CCSDS HISTORICAL DOCUMENT') headed the same entry 'Ingest Functional Entity' with 'contains the services and functions'; M-3 reads 'provides'. OAIS has no term for K2 or K3: zero hits for 'accession', 'appraisal', 'deaccession', 'retention' in M-3.                                                                                                                                                       |
| Submission Information Package (SIP)                                                  | digital preservation (OAIS)                        | Submission Information Package (SIP): An Information Package that is delivered by the Producer to the OAIS for use in the construction or update of one or more AIPs and/or the associated Descriptive Information.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | CCSDS 650.0-M-3 (OAIS), December 2024, https://public.ccsds.org/Pubs/650x0m3.pdf; §1.6.2 Terminology, p. 1-14                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | K6: 'or update of one or more AIPs' means a re-delivered SIP is also the OAIS vehicle for refreshing a stored copy.                                                                                                                             | The SIP is the unit that moves during K1; in vault terms the Zotero record plus attachments is the SIP. Standard across the field; Archivematica and Preservica both use the term.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Producer                                                                              | digital preservation (OAIS)                        | Producer: The role played by those persons or client systems that provide the information to be preserved. This can include internal or external OAIS persons or systems.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | CCSDS 650.0-M-3 (OAIS), December 2024, https://public.ccsds.org/Pubs/650x0m3.pdf; §1.6.2 Terminology, p. 1-13                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | None found.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Names the Zotero-library side of K1: the vault is the OAIS and the local Zotero library is the Producer. Preservica's OPEX documentation reuses Producer/Consumer in exactly this sense.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Ingest                                                                                | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                     | Ingest: Process of adding objects to a Preservation Repository's storage system. In the context of OAIS, Ingest includes services and functions that accept Submission Information Packages (SIP) from producers, and transform them into one or more Archival Information Packages (AIP) for long-term retention.                                                                                                                                      | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0 (June 2015, revised November 2015), Library of Congress, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 270 (PDF p. 280)                                                                                                                                                                         | K4: PREMIS, like OAIS, folds SIP-to-AIP construction into Ingest.                                                                                                                                                                               | Standard; PREMIS explicitly defers to OAIS. PREMIS also defines 'Pre-Ingest: Period in the life cycle of a Digital Object before it is Ingested into a Preservation Repository.' (p. 271).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ingestion                                                                             | preservation metadata (PREMIS event types)         | The process of adding objects to a preservation repository. More detail can be gained by utilizing "Ingestion Start" and "Ingestion End" rather than this one event.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Library of Congress, PREMIS Event Type controlled vocabulary, http://id.loc.gov/vocabulary/preservation/eventType/ing; skos:definition on concept URI .../eventType/ing (fetched as ing.json; the HTML listing returns 403 to non-browser clients)                                                                                                                                         | None found.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Standard controlled value used in PREMIS eventType. Companion values: 'ingestion start' = 'The process of starting the ingest process.' (.../ins) and 'ingestion end' = 'The process of completion of the total ingest process.' (.../ine).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| transfer                                                                              | preservation metadata (PREMIS event types)         | The process of transmitting metadata and/or digital object(s) to a repository.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Library of Congress, PREMIS Event Type controlled vocabulary, http://id.loc.gov/vocabulary/preservation/eventType/tra; skos:definition on concept URI .../eventType/tra                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K1 (SAA 'transfer'): the records-management custody move. K1 (Archivematica 'transfer'): the pre-SIP stage.                                                                                                                                     | Standard controlled value. It names the move across the repository boundary without the quality-assurance and packaging connotations of ingest.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Capture                                                                               | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                     | Capture: Process by which a Preservation Repository actively obtains Digital Objects for long-term retention, for example, a harvesting program that collects Web Sites. Note that the Capture process precedes the Ingest process.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 267 (PDF p. 277)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | K4: the vault's working label 'capture' names the deterministic copy step; in PREMIS capture is acquisition-side pull that precedes ingest, so it sits on the K1 side.                                                                          | Standard in PREMIS. Its position is the reverse of the vault's working label: PREMIS capture is how the repository actively fetches material from outside (web harvesting), not a post-acceptance copy with provenance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| capture                                                                               | preservation metadata (PREMIS event types)         | The process whereby a repository actively obtains an object through means other than a transfer from the creator/donor.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Library of Congress, PREMIS Event Type controlled vocabulary, http://id.loc.gov/vocabulary/preservation/eventType/cap; skos:definition on concept URI .../eventType/cap                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K4: the working label 'capture'.                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Standard controlled value; defined by contrast with 'transfer' (push from creator) as pull by the repository. Agent-driven fetching of a source into Zotero on the human's instruction matches this sense more than K4 does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ingest                                                                                | archival science (SAA)                             | ingest n. the process of accepting electronic content or metadata into an electronic repository or database                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/ingest.html; entry 'ingest', headword definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | K4: the SAA citations quote Lavoie 2014 describing ingest as spanning receipt, validation, transformation, metadata extraction and transfer to the archival store.                                                                              | Standard; SAA treats it as the digital-repository counterpart of accessioning (one citation calls it 'the ingest or digital accessioning process').                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| accession (n.)                                                                        | archival science (SAA)                             | accession n. the materials physically and officially transferred to a repository as a unit at a single time                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/accession.html; entry 'accession', first (noun) sense                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | K3: the verb sense of the same headword. K7: 'accession number' is the identifier assigned to this unit.                                                                                                                                        | Standard in archives. The noun names the batch that arrives (the K1-side unit of transfer); the verb names the act of taking custody (K3). The SAA entry also gives a second noun sense 'an acquisition'. No slug exists for 'accessioning' (404); the gerund is covered by the verb sense.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| acquisition                                                                           | archival science (SAA)                             | acquisition n. materials physically and officially transferred to a repository as a unit at a single time                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/acquisition.html; entry 'acquisition', headword definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | K1 'accession (n.)': identical wording in SAA.                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Standard. In practice 'acquisition' is the broader collecting activity (including solicitation, purchase, gift) while 'accession' is the formal intake unit; SAA's headword definitions are word for word the same.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| transfer                                                                              | archival science (SAA, records management sense)   | transfer n. The process of moving records as part of their scheduled disposition, especially from an office to a records center, or from a records center to an archives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/transfer.html; entry 'transfer', headword definition; Notes: 'Transfer may involve a change in custody without a change in title.'                                                                                                                                                                         | K8: SAA ties transfer to 'scheduled disposition', the retention and disposition lifecycle.                                                                                                                                                      | Standard in records management. The archival sense is a custody move, not a copy; the vault copies rather than moves.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| transfer                                                                              | digital preservation software (Archivematica)      | In Archivematica, transfer is the process of moving any set of digital objects into Archivematica and turning the materials into a Submission Information Package (SIP). The Transfer tab prepares your content for preservation in Archivematica.                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Archivematica 1.18.0 documentation, User manual > Transfer, https://www.archivematica.org/en/docs/archivematica-1.18/user-manual/transfer/transfer/; page 'Transfer', opening paragraph                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K1/K4 boundary: Archivematica splits OAIS Ingest into 'transfer' (get material in, form the SIP) and 'ingest' (SIP to AIP), so Archivematica 'ingest' is narrower than OAIS, PREMIS and SAA 'ingest'.                                           | Product-specific usage in a widely deployed tool. Archivematica has no glossary page in 1.13 through 1.18 (all 404).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ingest                                                                                | digital preservation software (Preservica)         | POST to an SO or to /root to upload a package for ingest. This is equivalent to running the Standard Ingest workflow in that folder, and selecting the package file that you upload.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Preservica API Documentation (product documentation served from Preservica instances), https://demo.preservica.com/api/documentation.html; section on uploading content / Long Running Tasks                                                                                                                                                                                               | K4: Preservica 'ingest' is the whole intake including AIP formation; an 'Ingest' event type is recorded on export per the OPEX doc: 'On export, properties include Ingest, Link, Characterise, Re-Characterise, Migrate, UpdateProperties etc.' | Preservica publishes no public glossary; its user guides sit behind the customer portal. The developer site (OPEX doc) states the format 'is the principal mechanism used by Preservica for ingest and export' and that Preservica 'is the Consumer' on ingest, reusing OAIS Producer/Consumer roles. Usage follows OAIS.                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| add items / Adding Items                                                              | reference managers (Zotero)                        | This page describes the various ways to add items (e.g., books, journal articles, web pages, etc.) as items in Zotero. To learn more about adding files (such as PDFs or images), please see the files page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | https://www.zotero.org/support/adding_items_to_zotero; Adding Items to Zotero (intro); the old URL /support/getting_stuff_into_your_library 301-redirects here                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | K3: the same verb names the single record-creation act.                                                                                                                                                                                         | Zotero's umbrella verb for the whole intake process is 'add' (nav: 'Adding to Your Library'). Shared across the field (Citavi 'Adding References', Mendeley 'Add References', JabRef 'Add entry').                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| import                                                                                | reference managers (Zotero)                        | Zotero can import bibliographic data stored in a variety of standardized formats used by databases and other reference management tools. The most popular formats are RIS, Bib(La)Tex, and MODS. If you have a database stored in one of these formats, such as a BibTeX database you’ve compiled or a RIS database you’ve exported from another reference manager, you can import them into Zotero by clicking File → “Import…” and choosing “A file”. | https://www.zotero.org/support/kb/importing_standardized_formats; How do I import BibTeX or other standardized formats?                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K3. K4: import/export pair.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | In all six tools 'import' means file or database ingestion, paired with 'export'. Zotero's connector page also uses the verb for browser saves ('The quality of the data Zotero imports is determined by the information supplied on the webpage'), so even at the source 'import' covers both file loads and browser saves. #### K2: the human decision that a source is accepted into the library

Working label: admission.

| Preservation Description Information (PDI)                              | digital preservation (OAIS)                   | Preservation Description Information (PDI): The information, which along with Representation Information, is necessary for adequate preservation of the Content Data Object and which can be categorized as Provenance Information, Context Information, Reference Information, Fixity Information, and Access Rights Information. It is a type of Information Object.                                                                                                                                                                                | CCSDS 650.0-M-3 (OAIS), December 2024, https://public.ccsds.org/Pubs/650x0m3.pdf; §1.6.2 Terminology, p. 1-12                                                                                                                                                                                        | K7: Reference Information is one of the five PDI categories. | Provenance Information                                                  | digital preservation (OAIS)                   | Provenance Information: The information that documents the history of the Content Data Object. This information tells the origin or source of the Content Data Object, any changes that may have taken place since it was originated, and who has had custody of it since it was originated. The Archive is responsible for creating and preserving Provenance Information from the point of Ingest; however, earlier Provenance Information should be provided by the Producer. Provenance Information adds to the evidence to support Authenticity. | CCSDS 650.0-M-3 (OAIS), December 2024, https://public.ccsds.org/Pubs/650x0m3.pdf; §1.6.2 Terminology, p. 1-13                                                                                                                                                                                        | K6: 'any changes that may have taken place since it was originated' is what a drift check appends to.                                                        | ISO-standard term. M-2 (2012) read 'Content Information' where M-3 reads 'Content Data Object' in the Fixity, Provenance and Reference definitions. The split of responsibility is explicit: pre-ingest provenance comes from the Producer (Zotero), post-ingest provenance from the Archive (vault).                                                                          |
| Digital Provenance                                                      | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                | Digital Provenance: Documentation of processes in a Digital Object's life cycle. #### K5: the LLM step that turns a captured source into wiki pages

Working label: digest.

#### K6: detecting that the library-side record changed, vanished, or was re-keyed after capture, and refreshing the vault copy

Working labels: drift check, refresh, sync.

| Term                                                                         | Field                                         | Verbatim definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Source and where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Collisions                                                                                                                                                                       | Standardness note                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Fixity Check                                                                 | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                | Fixity Check: Process of verifying that a File or Bitstream has not been changed during a given period. A common Fixity Check method is to compute a Message Digest ("hash") at one point and recalculate the Message Digest at a later point; if the digests are identical, the object has not been altered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 270 (PDF p. 280)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | K4: the baseline digest is computed at capture.                                                                                                                                  | Standard. The direction is the reverse of K6: a fixity check verifies that the archive's own stored copy is unchanged; K6 detects that the producer-side record (Zotero) changed, vanished or was re-keyed.                                                                                                                                              |
| fixity check                                                                 | preservation metadata (PREMIS event types)    | The process of verifying that an object has not been changed in a given period.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Library of Congress, PREMIS Event Type controlled vocabulary, http://id.loc.gov/vocabulary/preservation/eventType/fix; skos:definition on concept URI .../eventType/fix                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | K4.                                                                                                                                                                              | Standard controlled value. Companion: 'message digest calculation' = 'The process by which a message digest ("hash") is created.' (.../mes).                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Fixity                                                                       | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                | Fixity: Property of a Digital Object that indicates it has not changed between two points in time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 270 (PDF p. 280)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | None found.                                                                                                                                                                      | Standard. SAA agrees: 'fixity n. the property of being unchanged' (https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/fixity.html). Fixity is a property of the stored object, so 'drift' in the vault's sense (upstream changed) is the negation of fixity on the producer side, which the field does not name.                                                    |
| Checking fixity (Storage Service)                                            | digital preservation software (Archivematica) | Checking fixity is the process of ensuring that digital files have not changed unexpectedly over time. The Archivematica Storage Service exposes an API endpoint that enables users to check the fixity of individual AIPs in storage. Users can access the API endpoint directly or by using the Fixity application developed by Artefactual. Archivematica AIPs are packaged according to the BagIt File Packaging Format. The fixity API endpoint verifies the bag by validating the contents of the bag against the checksums and payload oxum listed in the bag files. This can tell users if files have been modified, deleted, or added to the AIP. | Archivematica Storage Service 0.24 documentation, Fixity, https://www.archivematica.org/en/docs/storage-service-0.24/fixity/; page 'Fixity', opening section; results appear 'in the Fixity Date and Fixity Status columns. The fixity status is displayed as either "Success" or "Failed".'                                                                                                                                                                                    | K4.                                                                                                                                                                              | Product usage consistent with PREMIS. 'modified, deleted, or added' is the field's wording for the three outcomes that K6 calls 'changed, vanished, or re-keyed'; it is applied to the stored AIP, not to the source system.                                                                                                                             |
| Re-ingest AIP                                                                | digital preservation software (Archivematica) | There are three different types of AIP re-ingest: 1. Note that the original metadata will still be present but if you scroll down you'll also see another dmdSec that says STATUS="updated" [...] 2. Partial re-ingest This method is typically used by institutions who want to create a DIP sometime after they've made an AIP. [...] 3. Full re-ingest This method is for institutions who want to be able to run all the major microservices                                                           | Archivematica 1.18.0 documentation, User manual > Ingest > Re-ingest AIP, https://www.archivematica.org/en/docs/archivematica-1.18/user-manual/ingest/ingest/#reingest; section 'Re-ingest AIP'; also Archival storage page: 'From the AIP information page, it is possible to reingest an AIP in order to add or update metadata, create a DIP on demand, or re-run all microservices.'                                                                                        | K1/K4: re-ingest reruns the intake on an existing package. In this field 'refresh' means replacing storage media. Working labels: citekey, item key, identity.

#### K8: a source that was accepted but later excluded, superseded, or removed, with the record kept

Working labels: screening state, tombstone.

| Term                                                                                                  | Field                                            | Verbatim definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Source and where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Collisions                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Standardness note                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| deaccession                                                                                           | archival science (SAA)                           | deaccession n. the process by which an archives, museum, or library permanently removes accessioned materials from its holdings                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/deaccession.html; entry 'deaccession', noun sense; verb sense: 'v. to remove archival resources from intellectual and physical custody'                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | K3: the exact inverse of 'accession'. K2/K8 'reappraisal': the decision that precedes it.                                                                                                                   | Standard; the archival term for K8's transition. SAA's sense stresses permanence and removal from custody, whereas the vault keeps the record; PREMIS below separates inventory from storage.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Deaccession                                                                                           | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                   | Deaccession: Process of removing a Digital Object from the inventory of a Preservation Repository.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 268 (PDF p. 278); body text on Events: 'For example, a repository might first deaccession an Intellectual Entity, then delete all file objects associated with that entity, and record each deletion as an event.' (Data model section, paragraph before 'Identifiers', p. 16)                                                                      | K8 'Deletion': PREMIS deliberately separates removal from inventory from removal from storage.                                                                                                              | Standard. PREMIS deaccession changes the object's status in the inventory while the object and its events can persist, which is 'excluded, superseded, or removed, with the record kept'.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Deletion                                                                                              | preservation metadata (PREMIS)                   | Deletion: Process of removing a Digital Object from repository storage.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | PREMIS Data Dictionary for Preservation Metadata, version 3.0, https://www.loc.gov/standards/premis/v3/premis-3-0-final.pdf; Glossary, p. 268 (PDF p. 278)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K8 'Deaccession'.                                                                                                                                                                                           | Standard. K8 is deaccession without deletion; the distinction keeps 'tombstone' (record kept) separate from physical removal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| deaccession                                                                                           | preservation metadata (PREMIS event types)       | The process of formally removing an object from the inventory of a repository. (.../del).                                                                                               | Standard controlled value; enumerates the outcomes (transfer, return, deletion) so a K8 state can carry a reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| reappraisal                                                                                           | archival science (SAA)                           | reappraisal n. the process of identifying materials that no longer merit inclusion in an archives and that are candidates for deaccessioning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | SAA Dictionary of Archives Terminology, https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/reappraisal.html; entry 'reappraisal', headword definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | K2: the mirror decision to appraisal.                                                                                                                                                                       | Standard; names the human decision that moves a source into K8, which the working label 'screening state' conflates with the resulting state.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

It is sometimes used with the sense of a temporary loan, usually to the office of origin.'                                                                                                                                        | K8 'deaccession'; ambiguous with temporary loan.                                                                                                                                                            | Standard in libraries (and in institutional repositories, where a 'withdrawn' item keeps a tombstone record).
