'retention' alone has no SAA entry (404); it appears as 'retention period' and 'retention schedule' ('a document that identifies and describes an organization's records, usually at the series level, and provides instructions for the disposition of records throughout their life cycle', https://dictionary.archivists.org/entry/retention-schedule.html). Retention governs the timing of K8, not the state.                                                        |

| Deletion requested (Delete AIP)                                                                       | digital preservation software (Archivematica)    | Deleting an AIP in Archivematica is a two-step process. On the AIP information page, navigate to the Delete action tab at the bottom of the page. Enter the AIP UUID and a reason for deletion. Click delete. When you refresh the Archival Storage tab, the status of your AIP should now read Deletion requested.       | Archivematica 1.18.0 documentation, User manual > Archival storage > Delete AIP, https://www.archivematica.org/en/docs/archivematica-1.18/user-manual/archival-storage/archival-storage/; section 'Delete AIP'                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | K8 'deaccession' versus 'deletion': Archivematica implements deletion with approval and a recorded reason, and keeps no tombstone in the index after approval.                                              | Product-specific. ______________________________________________________________________

### Collisions

Terms that two or more fields use for different concepts. Each entry gives the quotes on each side. The vault's own working labels are noted where they land inside a collision.

#### import

- Reference managers, file load paired with export (K1): "Zotero can import bibliographic data stored in a variety of standardized formats used by databases and other reference management tools." (Zotero, importing_standardized_formats)
- Evidence synthesis, bulk pre-decision load (K1): "You can import references into Covidence by clicking on the Import Studies tool from your Review Summary page. Here you can select the file and the category (which is the stage or step of the review process) that the references will appear in." (Covidence, study-imports)
- Data engineering, bulk copy from a system of record, with an incremental mode that is a refresh (K1, K6): "Sqoop provides an incremental import mode which can be used to retrieve only rows newer than some previously-imported set of rows." (Sqoop User Guide 1.4.7, 7.2.10)
- LLM wiki, bulk corpora only (K1 subset): "Every collection import writes a manifest to `raw/repos/` tagged `collection-manifest`, plus one immutable child source per upstream page/spec in `raw/articles/`" (nvk/llm-wiki AGENTS.md)
- The vault's `CONTEXT.md` currently uses Import for the post-admission projection (K4): "The machine projection of an admitted item into the evidence layer — a literature note rendered, never authored." No field read uses the word for a step after acceptance.

#### source

- Scoping reviews, the document under review (the vault's 'source'): "Selection of sources of evidence† [...] † A more inclusive/heterogeneous term used to account for the different types of evidence or data sources (e.g., quantitative and/or qualitative research, expert opinion, and policy documents) that may be eligible in a scoping review as opposed to only studies. This is not to be confused with information sources (see first footnote)." (PRISMA-ScR item 9)
- Search reporting, the database or website searched: "Literature search: Here, an overall term for the entire information retrieval process as part of a systematic review. This includes the full range of searching methods and information sources, including databases, study registries, regulatory datasets, web searches, government documents, unpublished data, and much more." (PRISMA-S Table 2)
- LLM wiki, a provenance field holding the origin locator: raw frontmatter field 'source: "URL or filepath or MANUAL"' (nvk/llm-wiki AGENTS.md); llmwikis separately has 'source record' (raw-side metadata) and 'source page' ("Create a source page. Write wiki/sources/source-slug.md as the compact proxy for the raw file.")
- Data engineering, the upstream database: "Debezium is a set of distributed services that capture row-level changes in your databases" and "After a source record is deleted, emitting a tombstone event" (Debezium FAQ; PostgreSQL connector)
- OAIS, an input package: "Derived AIP: An AIP generated by extracting or aggregating information from one or more source AIPs." (CCSDS 650.0-M-3)

#### record

- Evidence synthesis, a database entry about a report, many per report (K7 level below the study): "Record —The title or abstract (or both) of a report indexed in a database or website (such as a title or abstract for an article indexed in Medline). Records that refer to the same report (such as the same journal article) are “duplicates”" (PRISMA 2020 Box 1)
- Archival science, the internal intake artifact created at accession (K3 output): "accession record n. a consistent and normally internal set of data detailing an archives' acquisition, documenting legal and physical transfer, and supporting description" (SAA)
- Reference managers, a row with a per-library sequential number: "EndNote assigns a unique record number to each reference as it is added to your library ." (EndNote, Record Numbers)
- Knowledge management, vault-side metadata beside the raw copy: "Ingest. Store the original in raw/, create a source record, extract claims, and mark uncertain or sensitive material." (llmwikis.org, Operate)
- Data engineering, a database row: "After a source record is deleted, emitting a tombstone event (the default behavior) allows Kafka to completely delete all events that pertain to the key of the deleted row" (Debezium PostgreSQL connector)

#### ingest

- OAIS and PREMIS, the whole intake from SIP acceptance to AIP construction (K1 and K4): "Ingest: Process of adding objects to a Preservation Repository's storage system. In the context of OAIS, Ingest includes services and functions that accept Submission Information Packages (SIP) from producers, and transform them into one or more Archival Information Packages (AIP) for long-term retention." (PREMIS v3 Glossary)
- Archivematica, only the SIP-to-AIP stage after 'transfer' (K4): "During ingest, digital objects are packaged into SIPs and run through several microservices, including normalization, packaging into an AIP and generation of a DIP." (Archivematica 1.18, Ingest)
- LLM wiki, the whole process including page writing (K1 through K5): "**Ingest.** You drop a new source into the raw collection and tell the LLM to process it. An example flow: the LLM reads the source, discusses key takeaways with you, writes a summary page in the wiki, updates the index, updates relevant entity and concept pages across the wiki, and appends an entry to the log." (Karpathy gist)
- LLM wiki, raw-file creation only (K4): "Convert external material into a raw source file." (nvk/llm-wiki AGENTS.md, Ingest)
- Knowledge management, the compilation step (K4 and K5): "Ingest is the compilation step. A new raw source enters the system, and the agent turns it into a source summary, updated concept pages, new links, contradiction records, index updates, and a log entry." (llmwikis.org, Ingest)
- Data engineering, the entry edge of a pipeline with no LLM step (K1 = K4): "Loading data in Druid is called ingestion or indexing." (Apache Druid, Ingestion overview)

#### capture

- PREMIS, acquisition-side pull that precedes ingest (K1): "Capture: Process by which a Preservation Repository actively obtains Digital Objects for long-term retention, for example, a harvesting program that collects Web Sites. Note that the Capture process precedes the Ingest process." (PREMIS v3 Glossary)
- Archival science, making surrogate copies such as scans: "capture n. a process that creates alternative copies of records" (SAA)
- LLM wiki, saving the raw copy with hash and provenance (K4, the vault's working label): "① **Capture the raw source:** - URL → use `web_extract` to get markdown, save to `raw/articles/` [...] **Add raw frontmatter** (`source_url`, `ingested`, `sha256` of the body)." (hermes-agent SKILL.md)
- Data engineering, capturing changes rather than records (K6): "Debezium is a set of distributed services that capture row-level changes in your databases so that your applications can see and respond to those changes." (Debezium FAQ)
- LLM wiki, recording a harness session: nvk 'Session capture is operational memory' (nvk/llm-wiki README)

#### sync

- Reference managers, bidirectional merge with conflict resolution: "When Zotero syncs, it automatically applies changes in both directions — any changes you make in one place will be applied to all other synced computers." (Zotero, sync)
- Reference managers, a one-way derived file the same vendor also calls export: "You can automatically sync a folder, label, or your complete Paperpile library to a BibTeX file in Google Drive, GitHub, or a downloadable web link." (Paperpile, sync-bibtex-files)
- Data engineering, an umbrella over full, incremental and CDC copying: "Data synchronization needs to support various synchronization scenarios such as offline-full synchronization, offline-incremental synchronization, CDC, real-time synchronization, and full database synchronization." (Apache SeaTunnel, About)
- LLM wiki, plugin or registry synchronisation unrelated to sources: 'repair the registry on the next sync', 'wikis.json sync', './scripts/sync-codex-plugin.sh' (nvk/llm-wiki README)

#### refresh

- OAIS, a bit-exact media copy: "Refreshment: A Digital Migration where the effect is to replace a media instance with a copy that is sufficiently exact that all Archival Storage hardware and software continues to run as before." (CCSDS 650.0-M-3)
- Better BibTeX, the act that regenerates citation keys (the re-key event K6 detects): "If you want your keys to update after a pattern change you will have to select your items, right-click, and select Refresh ." (BBT, citing)
- LLM wiki, the freshness check itself (K6): "Freshness check for wiki articles. Re-fetches source URLs, detects changes (cosmetic, additive, contradictory), and presents a human-gated assessment." (nvk/llm-wiki AGENTS.md, Refresh)

#### digest

- LLM wiki, a distilled note from an agent session: "Digests and feedback remain operational memory until an explicit `promote` writes a distilled note to a topic's `raw/notes/`." (nvk/llm-wiki README)
- Data engineering, a hash used to detect divergence between copies (K6 mechanism): "A digest read request is not a full read and only returns the hash value of the data." (Apache Cassandra, Read repair)
- LLM wiki, the same hash sense: 'sha256: <hex digest of the raw content below the frontmatter>' (hermes-agent SKILL.md)
- No source uses 'digest' for the source-to-pages step that the vault's working label names (K5).

#### key

- Zotero, the 8-character item identifier (K7): "<userOrGroupPrefix>/items/<itemKey> A specific item in the library" and example object "key": "ABCD2345" (Zotero Web API basics; write_requests)
- Zotero, an API credential: "Local API keys (Zotero 10+) are unrelated to zotero.org API keys and can’t be created in advance." (Zotero Local API, Authorizing Writes)
- BibTeX tooling, the human-readable citation key (K7): "Citation keys are unique identifiers for the references in your library. They allow you to easily cite sources in text-based writing and programming environments like LaTeX." (Paperpile, citation-keys)
- Data engineering, the record key across CDC events: "the PostgreSQL connector follows a delete event with a special tombstone event that has the same key but a null value." (Debezium PostgreSQL connector)

#### citation key

- Better BibTeX, JabRef and Paperpile, the BibTeX identifier: "BBT generates citation keys that take into account other existing keys in your library in a deterministic way, regardless of what part of your library you export, or the order in which you do it." (BBT, citing)
- Citavi, a style-driven abbreviated in-text citation, distinct from its 'BibTeX key': "Certain citation styles in the fields of mathematics and computer science require an abbreviated citation key for in-text citations. For example, this type of citation key might consist of the first letters of the last names of the first three authors and a two-digit year: [MiScWe09]." (Citavi manual 6)

#### label

- JabRef, a synonym for citation key: "The pattern used in the auto generation of citation labels can be set for each of the standard entry types" (JabRef, citationkeypatterns)
- EndNote, a user-owned identifier field: "The Label field can be used to manually enter any unique code that you would like to use for each record." (EndNote, Temporary Citations Preferences)
- Rayyan, a free tag that is explicitly not status: "Only exclusion reasons appear in the PRISMA flow diagram — labels do not." (Rayyan, Labels and Exclusion Reasons)

#### tombstone

- Kafka and Debezium, a marker that enables the key's history to be removed: "After a source record is deleted, emitting a tombstone event (the default behavior) allows Kafka to completely delete all events that pertain to the key of the deleted row in case log compaction is enabled for the topic." (Debezium PostgreSQL connector)
- Cassandra, a deletion marker with an expiry: "Cassandra treats a deletion as an insertion, and inserts a time-stamped deletion marker called a tombstone." and "At the end of its expiration period, the grace period, the tombstone is deleted as part of Cassandra’s normal compaction process." (Apache Cassandra, Compaction overview)
- The vault's working label for K8 uses the word for a record that is kept. In both data-engineering senses the marker exists so that the data can eventually be removed. No LLM-wiki, preservation, reference-manager or evidence-synthesis source read uses the word; Zotero's `GET /deleted?since=` list has the function without the name.

#### screening

- Evidence synthesis, an activity window between identification and inclusion: "Screening | Records screened (n = ) | Records excluded\*\* | Reports sought for retrieval | Reports not retrieved | Reports assessed for eligibility | Reports excluded" (PRISMA 2020 flow diagram); "There are five steps within the selection (also known as screening) of evidence sources." (JBI 10.2.7)
- The vault's working label 'screening state' uses the phase name for a lifetime status (unscreened, included, excluded, superseded). Reference managers do not use the word at all: Zotero's only contact is "If you need to review a large number of papers’ titles, authors, and abstracts (e.g., if you are conducting a systematic review" (Zotero, reports).

#### identity

- Knowledge management, provenance or attribution: "Check: Identity / Question: Can a reviewer tell where it came from and when? / Stop condition: No source title, origin, capture date, or path." (llmwikis.org, Ingest preflight)
- PREMIS, a repository-scoped identifier, which is the vault's working-label sense (K7): "A designation used to identify the Object uniquely within the preservation repository system in which it is stored." (PREMIS v3, objectIdentifier)

#### snapshot

- Zotero, a stored copy of a webpage attached as a child item: "a copy (or snapshot) of the webpage will be saved to your computer and added as a child item." (Zotero, adding_items_to_zotero)
- Data engineering, the initial consistent copy of the whole source before change streaming (K4 analogue): "the first time that the connector starts, it performs an initial consistent snapshot of the database." (Debezium PostgreSQL connector)
- LLM wiki, a Wayback capture: 'one raw article per readable snapshot' (nvk/llm-wiki ingestion.md)

#### transfer

- Archival science, a custody move under a disposition schedule: "transfer n. The process of moving records as part of their scheduled disposition, especially from an office to a records center, or from a records center to an archives." (SAA)
- PREMIS event types, transmission to a repository (K1): "The process of transmitting metadata and/or digital object(s) to a repository." (LOC eventType/tra)
- Archivematica, the pre-SIP stage (K1 front half): "In Archivematica, transfer is the process of moving any set of digital objects into Archivematica and turning the materials into a Submission Information Package (SIP)." (Archivematica 1.18, Transfer)
- Preservica OPEX, a metadata section: the Transfer section is 'Data to assist the assessment of whether the item is complete and correct.' (Preservica OPEX)

#### accession

- Archival science, noun, the batch that arrives (K1 unit): "accession n. the materials physically and officially transferred to a repository as a unit at a single time" (SAA)
- Archival science, verb, the act of taking custody (K3): "v. to take intellectual and physical custody of materials, often under legal or policy authority" (SAA)
- PREMIS event types, addition to the inventory (K3): "The process of adding an object to the inventory of a repository." (LOC eventType/acc)
- EndNote, an external database's identifier: "The Accession Number field is designed to contain the unique record number assigned to a reference from an online database ." (EndNote, Temporary Citations Preferences); SAA 'accession number' is instead the repository's own batch identifier: "A number or code assigned to uniquely identify a group of records or materials acquired by a repository" (SAA)

#### appraisal

- Archival science, the value decision before accession (K2): "appraisal n. the process of identifying materials offered to an archives that have sufficient value to be accessioned" (SAA)
- Evidence synthesis, quality assessment of research already selected: "uses systematic and explicit methods to identify, select, and critically appraise relevant research" (Cochrane Community glossary, Systematic review)

#### update

- Cochrane, a new edition of a review with new studies: "a new edition of a published systematic review with changes that can include new data, new methods, or new analyses to the previous edition" (Cochrane Handbook IV.1, quoting Garner et al 2016)
- Paperpile, overwriting a library record's metadata from external databases (a source of drift, not its detection): "Keep your reference metadata accurate and complete by automatically updating it from academic databases." (Paperpile, update-metadata-automatically)
- OAIS, revising a stored package through a re-delivered SIP: "An Information Package that is delivered by the Producer to the OAIS for use in the construction or update of one or more AIPs" (CCSDS 650.0-M-3, SIP)
- Archivematica, metadata-only re-ingest of an existing AIP: "This method is for adding or updating descriptive and/or rights metadata. Doing so will update the dmdSec of the AIP's METS file." (Archivematica 1.18, Re-ingest AIP)

#### identification

- PRISMA 2020, the phase in which records are found and loaded (K1): "Identification of studies via databases and registers | Records identified from\*: Databases (n = ) Registers (n = )" (PRISMA 2020 flow diagram)
- Covidence, the bibliographic block of an extraction form (K4 content): "The extraction form for a study has multiple sections; summary, identification, methods, population, interventions, outcomes and results data." (Covidence, data extraction)

#### provenance

- Archival science, origin of the records: "provenance n. the origin or source of something" (SAA)
- OAIS, the history of custody and change (K4's 'with provenance'): "Provenance Information: The information that documents the history of the Content Data Object. This information tells the origin or source of the Content Data Object, any changes that may have taken place since it was originated, and who has had custody of it since it was originated." (CCSDS 650.0-M-3)
- Data engineering, a per-datum processing lineage store: "As the data is processed through the system and is transformed, routed, split, aggregated, and distributed to other endpoints, this information is all stored within NiFi’s Provenance Repository." (Apache NiFi, Getting Started)
- Cochrane, the copied-versus-calculated distinction: "retain clear information on the provenance of the data, with a clear distinction between data from a source document and data obtained through calculations." (Cochrane Handbook 5.7)

#### archive

- OAIS, the repository organisation itself: "The Archive is responsible for creating and preserving Provenance Information from the point of Ingest" (CCSDS 650.0-M-3, Provenance Information)
- LLM wiki, a per-page disposition with the record kept (K8): "**Archive a page** when its content is fully superseded — move to `_archive/`, remove from index" (hermes-agent SKILL.md)
- LLM wiki, a whole-wiki context filter: "Archive is a context filter, not deletion: preserve the wiki, remove it from normal semantic workflows, and keep explicit restore/maintenance possible." (nvk/llm-wiki commands/archive.md)

#### merge

- Reference managers, resolving duplicate items into one master (K8 supersession): "You should always resolve duplicate items by merging them, rather than deleting one of the duplicates." (Zotero, duplicate_detection)
- Cochrane, combining search result files before dedup (K1/K3): "Merge search results from different sources using reference management software, and remove duplicate records of the same report" (Cochrane Handbook 4.6.3)
- Covidence, linking distinct reports of one study (K7): "References that have been merged are treated as a single study in your review and are displayed with a single Covidence ID number" (Covidence, Merging and un-merging studies); Covidence separates this from dedup: 'de-duplication in Covidence is distinct from merging , the process of linking multiple unique references which report on the same study'

#### deleted

- Zotero API, a kept list of removed keys (a tombstone in function). **Correction, 2026-09-05: this endpoint is documented for the Web API and is not served by the local API. `GET /api/users/0/deleted` and `?since=0` both return 404 on Zotero 10.0.1, live-probed. A local-API client cannot use it, which is why the ingest spec reads the whole key set instead.** "GET <userOrGroupPrefix>/deleted?since=<version> [...] { "collections": \[ "<collectionKey>" \], "searches": \[ "<searchKey>" \], "items": \[ "<itemKey>", "<itemKey>" \], "tags": \[ "<tagName>", "<tagName>" \] }" (Zotero Web API Syncing)
- Rayyan, a soft-delete status on a retained duplicate: "When you select a record to keep, the other record moves to Deleted status — it is not immediately and permanently erased." (Rayyan, Detect and Manage Duplicates)
- PREMIS, removal from storage: "Deletion: Process of removing a Digital Object from repository storage." (PREMIS v3 Glossary)

#### retract / retraction

- LLM wiki, deletion of a raw source and its derived claims for privacy or legal reasons (record not kept): "For a source path, map references, delete the raw source and unsupported derived claims, update indexes, write only a generic log entry" (nvk/llm-wiki AGENTS.md, Retract)
- EndNote, a publisher retraction flagged on a kept library record: "Retractions displays articles in the library which have been retracted. It works with Retraction Watch to check for new retractions and provide information on the reason for the retraction." (EndNote, Groups panel)
- Cochrane, a retracted study removed from analysis with the removal reported: "In those cases where data appear to be incorrect or possibly fabricated, they should be removed from the review analysis and this decision should be reported in the review." (Cochrane Handbook IV.4.1)

______________________________________________________________________

### Gaps

Every one of K1 through K8 has at least one primary-source term in at least one field, so no concept is unnamed everywhere. The gaps below are per field, and per facet of a concept where a field's term covers only part of it.

#### Fields with no term for a concept

- K2 (human acceptance decision): no term in reference managers (the decision is folded into the 'save' click, a 'Keep' checkbox, or an 'Add to project' button; none is a named state or event); no term in OAIS (acceptance is modelled once in the Submission Agreement, and 'appraisal' and 'accession' have zero hits in M-3); no term in data engineering (membership is a configuration-time include/exclude filter). 'admission' occurs once across all sources, in the llmwikis.org MATM agent-memory context, not for bibliographic sources.
- K3 (creating the library record): no term in evidence synthesis (records are retrieved from databases and bulk-imported; the singular act of authoring a record is not named; JBI allows records that never enter the reference manager at all); no separate term in LLM-wiki sources (dropping the file into raw/ is the record, so K3 collapses into K4; 'source record' in llmwikis is vault-side metadata); no term in PREMIS's own glossary (the 'accession' event lives only in the LOC vocabulary).
- K5 (LLM step to wiki pages): no term in digital preservation (Derived AIP, DIP, Descriptive Information and Derivation Relationship are structural neighbours, none names authored synthesis); no term in reference managers (Zotero 'Reports' renders metadata and notes, which is K4 output); no term in evidence synthesis that separates it from K4 (data collection, extraction and charting all describe judgement-laden reading; 'synthesis' and 'summary of evidence' operate on charted data; the field's name for any LLM step is 'automation tools'). Only the LLM-wiki sources name it ('compile'), and none of them uses 'digest' for it.
- K6 (producer-side drift): no field names the case where the source system's record changed, vanished or was re-keyed while the archive's copy stayed the same. Preservation 'fixity' checks the archive's own copy; OAIS 'Refreshment' is a media copy; reference-manager 'sync' is a bidirectional merge; Cochrane 'update' is a new edition and its IV.4.1 passage on corrections and retractions has no single term; PRISMA-S notes in one sentence that 'Citations already in the database may also be updated' and attaches no term; PRISMA's updated-review flow diagram has no box for a previously included study that changed or vanished. Data engineering 'change data capture' covers all three cases in a push model; the polling analogue, Sqoop 'incremental import', cannot see deletes or re-keys. hermes 'drift' and nvk 'refresh' check upstream URLs, not a library record. The Zotero local API supplies most of the mechanism (object versions, Server ID, `GET /deleted?since=`, `includeTrashed`) without a user-facing name.
- K7 (a report-level stable identifier): evidence synthesis keys identity at the study, links reports by registry numbers, and has no requirement that a report carry a stable key; DOI is the only report-level persistent identifier its glossaries define, and grey literature has none. LLM-wiki sources use the filename or slug as identity and none contains 'citekey' or 'item key'. The llmwikis 'identity' preflight means attribution, not an identifier.
- K8 ('accepted then later excluded, superseded, or removed, with the record kept'): evidence synthesis has no term; its excluded-studies tables cover near-misses never included, 'superseded' has no equivalent (a newer report is linked to the study rather than retiring the older one), and the accepted-then-removed case appears only as Cochrane IV.4.1 guidance with the removal reported. Reference managers have only Trash (time-limited, reversible, expiring) and EndNote's Retractions group. Both data-engineering 'tombstone' senses exist so that data can eventually be removed. 'screening state' occurs in no source. Preservation's PREMIS 'deaccession' (inventory) versus 'deletion' (storage) split is the one standard vocabulary that separates status change from removal.

#### Working labels found in no primary source with the vault's meaning

- 'import' for a post-admission projection (K4): every field places import before any decision.
- 'admission' for source acceptance: one occurrence, in a different context.
- 'capture' for the deterministic copy with provenance: used in that sense only by hermes-agent; PREMIS, SAA and Debezium each use it for something else.
- 'digest' for the LLM synthesis step: no occurrence; the dominant sense in both LLM-wiki and data-engineering sources is a hash.
- 'sync' for a one-way refresh of a derived copy: Paperpile and Mendeley do use 'sync' for one-way derived files, but every vendor's primary sense is bidirectional merge.
- 'identity' for a stable key: the one occurrence means attribution.
- 'screening state' as a lifetime status: no occurrence; 'screening' is a phase in every evidence-synthesis source.
- 'tombstone' for a kept record: no occurrence outside data engineering, where the marker's purpose is eventual removal.

#### Sources that could not be read

- Archivematica has no glossary page in versions 1.13 through 1.18, on the documentation site or in the docs repository.
- The Preservica user guides sit behind the customer portal; only developer documentation, the demo-instance API documentation, and one vendor marketing page were readable.
- The live Cochrane Handbook has no glossary; the Cochrane Community glossary was read from a 2019 Wayback snapshot.
- The PRISMA-S paper was readable only through the Europe PMC full-text XML; publisher pages returned no content.
- Mendeley's guide pages are JS-rendered and returned no content; Elsevier's support site was used instead.
- Clarivate's EndNote support articles returned no content; the docs.endnote.com product manual was used instead.
- Rayyan's help pages sit behind a Cloudflare challenge; the Zendesk API served the same articles.
- The SAA Dictionary has no entries for 'accessioning', 'deaccessioning', 'retention', 'persistent-identifier', 'submission', 'supersede', 'identifier' or 'unique-identifier' (all 404).

______________________________________________________________________

### Counts

Term rows: 244 (K1 34, K2 31, K3 21, K4 31, K5 20, K6 33, K7 32, K8 42). By vocabulary: digital preservation and archives 65, reference managers 62, evidence synthesis 71, LLM wiki, knowledge management and data engineering 46. Refuted rows: 0.
